It changes the optimizer's step: two views per iteration, where D-26 trains on one image, and a rule that combines their gradients per tensor, where §1.3 fixes the optimizer. The nearest class is a loss on today's primitives, which leaves that out (reading 1). Its opacity decay for conflicting primitives is a change to growth, relocation or split, of section 3.2's kind, driven by a signal that is this family's; its scaled densification gradient changes what E10's g accumulates. With the field off, E1 to E17 and the §3 tests are untouched | Two renders per iteration, with the two losses' gradients on the stored tensors kept apart: two backward passes (row 818). Nothing new from either backend (section 2.3) | The primitives are untouched. The conflict is read per primitive, on the gradients of position and opacity, the stored tensors `means` and `opacity_logits` (§1.1). A primitive that lives two frames is seen in few iterations, so the paper's running average over iterations has few samples of it. Fading by opacity meets E8 and relocation: a faded primitive is relocated onto a live one, not removed (E10, E11) | It names H2's mechanism at the optimizer's level: inconsistent observations give gradients from different views that point in conflicting directions on one primitive. It then does two things. Reconciling the gradients keeps both cameras' pull without letting one undo the other; the paper's own limitation is that this is a compromise between inconsistent signals that weakens fine structure, H2's blur. Fading the primitives that keep conflicting removes what the cameras disagree on, which is reading 2's case if that is the person. It assumes transient distractors and illumination changes over a static scene, with masks taking out most of the inconsistency beforehand (row 818) | None for the gradient part. Generators are not estimators and have no row: CAT4D's and CAT3D's diffusion models, the Stable Diffusion XL model ViDAR fine-tunes, the video models behind T2Bs, AniGS, 4Real, RIGI and EG4D, and SPIn-NeRF's 2D inpainter make the views or the content the technique is then trained on. Private scene A's views are given, and no row needs a generator but 4Real's distillation stage, which its row sets aside. Body templates are recorded with the hand-overs and not screened (decision 1). The estimators the rows cite from section 3.3 are SAM and SAM 2 (pass) and MASt3R (fail).

**Counts and the stop rule.** Twenty-five search rounds and one hand-over, all on 2026-11-15: 55 abstracts read, of the cap's 120, and 24 papers read in full, the cap. The screening table has 27 rows from 23 of those papers: WildGaussians, ViDAR and AligNeRF give two rows each, the row on known masks draws on two papers that have rows of their own, and one full read, 4DAnyone, gave a finding on scoring and no row. Three arXiv queries returned no result and are not rounds: the two row 816 names, and `multi-view video dynamic Gaussian splatting cameras color inconsistency calibration per-camera`, whose subject round 25 took up as a web search.

The stop rule is two consecutive rounds that add no technique passing the abstract screen. It was first met at rounds 19 and 20. Both probed one item of the ticket's six, generated images, and returned mostly papers already read, while two items had had one round each (weighting by trust, round 16; leaving pixels out, round 15). So the search went on: rounds 21 to 23 each added a technique (two models' co-pruning, gradient conflict, primitives per view), and rounds 24 and 25 added none. The search stopped at round 25, at 53 abstracts and 22 full reads. Two papers that ticket #10 handed over were read afterwards (the log's row 26), which brought the full reads to the cap. Each item had at least one round: generated images (rounds 2 to 5, 14, 17, 19, 20); robust and uncertainty-weighted losses (1, 13); correction per camera (7 to 11, 25); weighting cameras or pixels by trust (16, 21, 24); leaving out pixels known to be wrong (15); feature-space losses (12, 15, 22).

**The search seeds.** Each has its row. CAT4D: its own answer to inconsistency is on the generator's side, and its reconstruction adds a perceptual term and a lower weight for generated images (row 794). SpotLessSplats: one row. WildGaussians: two rows, the uncertainty and the appearance model.

**The recency round** is rounds 18 to 20, restricted to 2025-10 onward. Round 18 is the list of the papers that cite FreeTimeGS: 72 on Semantic Scholar on 2026-11-15, 69 of them dated 2025-10 or later. Eight abstracts were read and two papers in full, GraphiXS and Splannequin, which have rows; neither concerns cameras that disagree, GraphiXS treating missing data and Splannequin one camera. Three more of the citing papers bear on this family and answer on the generator's side: StudioRecon, met in round 2, which says video diffusion is geometrically inconsistent for people and keeps generated views for the background alone; MV-Performer; and Flex4DHuman. No title among the 72 names training on views that disagree; Phase-DGS, on cameras out of step, is the nearest. Rounds 19 and 20 added no technique. Outside those rounds, four papers read in full are of the same period: PDF-GS (2026-04), HarmoGS (2026-05), 4DAnyone (2026-08) and FreeTimeGS++ (2026-05), which is on the citing list, was handed over by ticket #10 and was read in its section 5.3 alone. FastWild-GS (2026-11) was read as an abstract.

**Met and not read in full,** by the mechanism whose row stands for them. Abstracts only (rows 785 to 793, 815 to 817, 823, 825):

- **Masks from residuals with pretrained features or segmentation,** as SpotLessSplats' and WildGaussians' rows: "Robust 3D Gaussian Splatting for Novel View Synthesis in Presence of Distractors" (arXiv 2408.11697), NeRF On-the-go (2405.18715), Sparse View Distractor-Free Gaussian Splatting (2603.01603), MVGSR (2503.08093), RU4D-SLAM (2602.20807). DF3DV-1K (2604.13416) is a benchmark of nine such methods.
- **Content split off into primitives of its own,** as DeSplat's row: DeGauss (2503.13176), HybridGS (2412.03844).
- **Two models,** as RIGI's and CoR-GS' rows: Asymmetric Dual 3DGS (2506.03538). Its abstract adds a caution those rows need: two models can collapse into the same failure, and it gives them different masks to prevent it.
- **Confidence against input views taken as reliable:** ConFixGS (2605.09688), AdaGS (2602.21535). They assume trusted cameras, which private scene A lacks (decision 4).
- **Colour per camera,** as WildGaussians' second row and the bilateral grids': Gaussian-DK (2408.09130), FastWild-GS (2611.13808), and Robust Gaussian Splatting (2404.04211), which also refines poses.
- **Poses:** JOintGS (2602.04317), which anchors the cameras on the static background and fits a body template; CG-3DGS (2408.08723), a loss on correspondences between render and image.
- **Timing:** Phase-DGS (2611.04666), which ticket #10 also handed to this family. Frame offsets explain none of private scene A's disagreement (section 2.5), so its premise does not hold here.
- **The generator's side,** which this family records and does not screen, private scene A's views being given: StudioRecon (2607.09125), Diffuman4D (2507.13344), Flex4DHuman (2606.13655), which ticket #10 also handed over as the kind of input private scene A is (one video turned into multi-view video, then an off-the-shelf 4D Gaussian stage), MV-Performer (2510.07190), Diffusion4D (2405.16645), 4DHumanDiff (2607.27634), GaussVid (2608.21849), 3D-GIMP (2607.20789), GeoGS3D (2403.10242) and GenMOJO (2506.12716).

**Techniques handed to other families.** Each is named with the family whose section holds, or would hold, its row. This section screened none of them.

- **Section 3.1, family A:** MVGSR's multi-view consistency loss for surfaces (row 785).
- **Section 3.2, family B:** SpotLessSplats' utilization-based pruning (row 796); AniGS' anisotropy regularizer (row 801); the L1 regularizers on opacity and on scale that 3R-GS and GraphiXS take from 3DGS-MCMC (rows 809, 820); "Dusting the Phantom", on where relocated primitives go (arXiv 2610.21302), and "Clustered Error Correction with Grouped 4D Gaussian Splatting" (arXiv 2511.16112), both family D's as well (rows 815, 825). HarmoGS' opacity decay and CoR-GS' co-pruning keep their rows here, because their signal is this family's; the decay and the pruning are of family B's kind.
- **Section 3.4, family C, part 2:** AniGS' L1 loss on rendered normals against normal maps (row 801); SPIn-NeRF's depth term (row 811); the depth, flow and tracking priors of ViDAR's base method (row 799).
- **Section 3.5, family D:** USplat4D, an uncertainty per Gaussian from how often it is observed (arXiv 2510.12768); by title only, from the list of papers that cite FreeTimeGS, SharpTimeGS (2602.02989), 4DSurf (2603.28064) and Constrained Dynamic Gaussian Splatting (2602.03538) (row 815); the rest of FreeTimeGS++ (2605.03337), of which this section read the colour correction alone (row 829); GraphiXS' dynamics of higher order (row 820); T2Bs' control points, a motion shared between primitives (row 800). Splannequin keeps its row here and is family D's in kind.
- **Body templates,** recorded and not searched for (decision 1): AniGS' mesh from SMPL-X, JOintGS' body poses, 4DAnyone's skeletons.

**What the family finds.** Five things, for section 4. None of them picks a finalist.

1. **The nearest published setting answers on the generator's side.** 4DAnyone generates views of a person from one video and then trains FreeTimeGS on them, changing nothing in that training in the parts read; CAT4D makes its views consistent while sampling them; both then reconstruct plainly (rows 794, 826). What is said of 4DAnyone here and in point 5 rests on the authors' reproduction of FreeTimeGS, in that paper's case its authors' own FreeTimeGS runs; it is the design of an evaluation, with no figure and no set-up taken. No paper read trains on views of which none is trusted and whose subject differs in every one. Every weighting by origin (CAT3D, CAT4D, ViDAR, ConFixGS) leans on real views, and every method that takes one view or time as the reference (T2Bs, AniGS, 4Real, EG4D) has a source to take, the view the others were generated from; private scene A's centre camera was regenerated like the others (decision 4).
2. **The diagnosis is shared, and it is H2's.** Trained naively on inconsistent generated views, a model blurs or overfits single frames (ViDAR); densifies redundant points to satisfy some viewpoints (RIGI); fits the noise by per-frame deformation and more Gaussians (EG4D); receives conflicting gradients on one primitive (HarmoGS). The price of the cure is shared too: lowering the weight where views disagree trades artifacts for blur, by RIGI's, HarmoGS' and CAT3D's own accounts.
3. **By part of the setting.**
   - *The background's part:* a pose correction per camera (ViDAR's first row, 3R-GS, 4Real). Two cautions: joint pose optimization blurs a scene part of which differs subtly between views (row 810), so the update should see the background only; and the best practice read ends at 0.2° (row 809), the size of the error measured.
   - *The person's part,* three kinds of answer. A deformation per camera (T2Bs, AniGS, 4Real) gives each view its own geometry and keeps one; all three take a reference view. A lower weight, by four signals: the residual (RobustNeRF, SpotLessSplats) and pretrained features (WildGaussians, PDF-GS) would mark the person, in every camera and frame, and remove it (reading 2); two models' disagreement (RIGI, CoR-GS) and gradient conflict per primitive (HarmoGS) trust no camera and do not decide beforehand what is wrong. And an outlet or a moved target: primitives per view outside the model (DeSplat), a loss that lets each patch shift (AligNeRF), a perceptual term alone inside the region that disagrees (SPIn-NeRF), detail withheld (EG4D).
   - *Placeholder pixels:* known masks left out of the loss; nothing else in the family is needed for them.
   - *Colour:* an affine transform per camera (Dynamic 3D Gaussians, FreeTimeGS++), bilateral grids or appearance embeddings, for a part nobody has measured. FreeTimeGS++'s is the one row of the family proposed for FreeTimeGS's own primitives, and its evidence rests on the authors' reproduction of FreeTimeGS.
4. **What needs no spec change, and what needs the least.** None: other values of `loss.lambda_perc` and `loss.perc_net`, where D-89 already holds two DINOv2 networks and section 2.1 records one trial, of another network; a correction of the manifest's cameras, once, before training; AligNeRF's re-alignment of the images, which alters the data. The least: a mask on training images, which changes §2.1's `total_loss` and §2.2's manifest and nothing else (reading 2).
5. **Test views.** Every correction per camera changes what E16's score means (reading 3), and the papers read meet that by fitting the correction on the test image (WildGaussians' embedding, the bilateral grids' affine alignment, 3R-GS' test poses) or by warping the test images onto the model (AligNeRF). FreeTimeGS++ avoids the question: its colour correction acts in the training losses only, and the final renders are made without it. 4DAnyone reads the score of held-out generated views as a measure of the views' consistency, not of the reconstruction (row 826): under decision 4 that is what the notes' 24.1 dB against 28.1 dB measures (section 2.1).

**For ticket #13:** the techniques this ticket would look at more closely, and why. Input, not a decision.

- **Known masks left out of the loss.** The one technique whose defect is measured, whose masks exist and whose assumption holds (section 2.5); the smallest spec change of the family.
- **A camera correction from background matches,** made once before training. It answers the background's part, which section 2.5 measured and removed with turns of 0.1° to 0.4°, and changes no spec item; ViDAR's and 3R-GS' ablations say what the trained form adds, AligNeRF what to keep it away from.
- **A perceptual term alone on the person's crop** (SPIn-NeRF's split), with other values of `loss.lambda_perc` and `loss.perc_net` as its form without a spec change. A loss on today's primitives, which review 1 asks for at least once; its stated failure, views that disagree in more than texture, is the risk.
- **AligNeRF's alignment-aware loss.** The only loss read that tolerates shifts of the size measured without trusting a view; its evidence is small and on a NeRF.
- **An affine colour correction per camera, in the training losses only** (FreeTimeGS++'s form). Cheap, proposed for these primitives, and it leaves E16's score as it is; but it answers colour, which nobody has measured on private scene A, and its evidence rests on the authors' reproduction of FreeTimeGS. A measurement of the cameras' colour differences would say whether it has anything to absorb.
- **The two signals that trust no camera:** two models' disagreement (RIGI, CoR-GS) and gradient conflict per primitive (HarmoGS). Before either is tried, the two default checkpoints of section 2.6, which differ by seed alone, can say without training whether two models disagree where the shell is (RIGI's and CoR-GS' rows).
- **A deformation per camera, held small** (4Real's form), and **primitives per view outside the model** (DeSplat). They absorb the person's part explicitly, where the shell absorbs it inside the model; they are the largest changes of the family, they need an answer to the missing reference camera, and they change what a test view's score means.

## 4. The finalists and review 1

*Ticket #13's section.* One screening table and one estimators table, consolidated from sections 3.1 to 3.6; H2's premise, answered with the probes' figures (sections 2.5 to 2.7); two to four finalists, at least one of them only a loss on today's primitives, each with the reason it was chosen; the proposed criteria for training tests, starting from the PRD's proposal; per finalist, the route for trying it and its GPU hours; the owner's answers at review 1, with their date.

Written on 2026-11-16 from sections 2.4 to 2.7 and 3.1 to 3.6 as merged. Nothing here was trained, rendered or measured anew, and no source was opened that those sections had not logged, so this section adds no source-log row. A figure is cited by the section that measured it, and a technique by its row in section 4.8: the letter is its family and the number its place in that family's screening table, so row E16 is the sixteenth row of section 3.6's table, where its detail and its source-log rows are. A statement marked "section 4's reading" is this ticket's own, from the tables of the sections it cites, and was not run. A finalist is a proposal to test, not a recommendation to adopt; section 7 recommends. Sections 4.1 to 4.7 are what review 1 decides on, and sections 4.8 and 4.9 are the two consolidated tables they cite.

### 4.1 What is proposed

**On H2's premise** (section 4.2). The cameras do disagree about the person, 21 to 99 times the measure's floor by camera pair, and a depth prior made per camera would inherit the disagreement wherever it is aligned to triangulated points. Two things qualify that. Part of the disagreement is not about the person: the scene manifest's cameras are off by 0.1° to 0.4°, and two camera pairs that share a camera place the person 4 cm apart in depth, mostly by one fixed relation per camera. And nothing measured ties the shell to the disagreement: what could be measured is millimetres to a few centimetres, on the fifth to a quarter of the person that the matcher can match, against a shell 5 to 25 cm off the body, and the notes' shell measures have never been taken on a scene whose cameras agree. So the premise holds, and H2 itself is untested. Only training can test it.

**Four finalists,** in the order proposed for trying them (section 4.3). Each is a proposal to test.

| | Finalist | Rows of section 4.8 | Integration class | What its test would show | Route | GPU hours |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The cameras corrected once, before training, from background matches | E6's variant, E15 | A preprocessing of the scene manifest; no spec item changes | H2 by removal: for the background's part for certain, and for the 4 cm between pairs only if its first step shows that a fit reaches it | A corrected manifest, then `ftgs init roma` and `ftgs train` unchanged | 2, or 4 with its control on `kitchen` |
| 2 | An alignment-tolerant pixel loss | E16 | A loss on today's primitives | H2 by removal, for the person's part: whether the short-lived primitives go when the loss absorbs shifts of a few pixels, and what it costs in sharpness | A scratch script | 2 to 4 |
| 3 | Surface terms without a prior: flattening, then depth–normal consistency | A1, A3, A5 | A loss on today's primitives, then a loss that needs new render outputs | H1's constraint alone: whether it removes the shell, or trades it for blur as H2 predicts | A scratch script on Q-226's device | 6 |
| 4 | A normal prior, on top of finalist 3's flattening | C7, C8 | A loss that needs new render outputs, with one estimator | H1 as the owner worded it, with the one kind of prior that inherits neither the 4 cm nor a scale and shift | A scratch script on the device; the prior maps as files | 4.5, or 6.5 with a second estimator |

Finalist 2 is only a loss on today's primitives, and so is the first arm of finalist 3. None needs a kernel gsplat 1.5.3 lacks, median depth or the pairwise distortion. Finalists 1 to 3 need no estimator; finalist 4's, MoGe-2's normal model, passes the bar, as does DAViD's, its alternative made for people. With a shared baseline of 2.5 hours the four come to 17 to 19 GPU hours on one RTX 4090, or 25 to 27 if a run on private scene A takes twice the notes' median; finalists 1 and 2 with the baseline come to 6.5 to 8.5, or 9.5 to 12.5 (section 4.5).

**How thin the evidence is.** For every finalist it is thin, and section 4.3 says under each how thin. No paper read trains on several cameras whose images disagree at one time with none of them trusted.

**Before any training,** six checks that need none (section 4.6, item 15). Two are new here and could take away the reason for finalists 1 and 2: the notes' measures on openftgs-synth's checkpoints, where the cameras agree, and the same measures on private scene A split at frame 80, after which five of the seven camera pairs disagree two to four times more.

**The criteria** (section 4.4) keep the PRD's shape and change it in ten places. The main ones: the two shell measures are reported apart, each on the near side and against one fixed init points file; sharpness is measured, since blur is what H2 predicts; a test-view score is a guard at 0.3 dB and not at the seed noise of 0.01 dB; on openftgs-synth the guard is the geometry residual's shares and SSIM₁ over three seeds, because one run cannot show "no worse" on the error of expected depth or on PSNR.

**What review 1 decides** is section 4.6's list: the four finalists, the criteria, the routes, the hours and their order, and items 5 to 18 of the PRD's comment of 2026-11-16, each with its options, a recommendation and what another answer would change. Two answers to the open items would change the shortlist: if a one-off correction of the manifest's cameras is out of scope, finalist 1 goes, and if flat primitives are wanted as a finalist, they take finalist 3's place and wait for a spec revision. The results of item 15's checks could change it too. The owner's answers go in section 4.7.

### 4.2 H2's premise, answered

H2 says that the shell is how training reconciles cameras whose images disagree, so that a surface constraint alone would trade the shell for blur, and a depth estimated on each camera would inherit the disagreement. The probes could test the two parts of its premise: that the cameras disagree about the person, and that a prior map made per camera inherits the disagreement. No probe tested H2 itself, that the shell is the reconciliation.

**What is shown.**

- **The cameras disagree about the person** (section 2.5). Inside the person's crop the dense matcher's admitted matches lie 1.84 px from epipolar agreement at the median and 6.61 px at the 90th percentile. openftgs-synth's `kitchen`, whose cameras agree exactly, reads 0.033 and 0.100 px on its dynamic objects at the same 15° between cameras, so a pair of private scene A reads 21 to 99 times the floor. The disagreement is uneven: in five of the seven camera pairs it is 2.2 to 4 times higher in frames 80 to 119, where the person is closest. Frame offsets explain none of it.
- **Part of the disagreement is the cameras', not the person's** (sections 2.5 and 2.7). The background, which does not move, reads 0.29 to 2.24 px, and a turn of one camera of each pair by 0.10° to 0.41° brings it to 0.28 to 0.60 px: the scene manifest's cameras are not quite the cameras the images show. The person's residual does not follow the turn; it stays at 0.88 to 3.96 px at the median, 2 to 13 times the background's whichever geometry is taken. In depth, two camera pairs that share a camera triangulate the same pixel of the person 4.0 % apart at the median, 4.1 cm, 27 times `kitchen`'s floor. Most of that follows one fixed relation per camera, such as wrong cameras would give; 1.0 % to 1.6 % remains and changes with the frame.
- **A depth prior per camera inherits the disagreement in position, whole** (section 2.7). A relative depth map has no scale or shift of its own, so it lands where its alignment puts it: the same map, aligned on the anchors of its camera's two pairs, is lifted to depths 4.2 % apart. One scale and one shift per image do not hold the person and the background together either: fitted to both, they leave the person 4.8 % off and the background 13.5 % off, with opposite signs.
- **Placeholder pixels are a separate, small defect, and the shell does not answer to them.** They are `cam0`'s alone: 0.263 % of its pixels, one in 225 of its crop, in every frame, where the matcher admits next to nothing (section 2.5). On section 2.6's figures the shell is the same whether `cam0` trains or not (section 4's reading; one run each, and the two runs also differ by one training camera): the nine-camera model `stride4` and `c3_s4`, the same configuration with `cam0` held out, have far-5 cm shares of 0.209 and 0.206 against the same init points file; 0.122 and 0.124 within the near side, each against its own file; and under-two-frames shares of 0.456 and 0.447.

**What is not shown.**

- **That the disagreement is of the shell's size.** Across the epipolar line the person's median is 2 to 10 mm per pair and the 90th percentile 5 to 27 mm (section 2.5). In depth it is 4 cm between pairs, and about 1 cm once the fixed relation is taken out (section 2.7). The shell sits 5 to 25 cm from the body. Both figures are lower bounds, taken on the part of the person that can be matched: 18 % of the crop's grid points, 27 % of the person's kept pixels. On the other 73 % the two cameras' lifted surfaces lie 2.4 to 2.6 cm apart at the median, with a tail that `kitchen` shows too, from occlusion.
- **That a prior map inherits a disagreement in shape.** Aligned pair by pair, two cameras' surfaces of the person lie 1.85 % to 2.02 % of the depth apart at the median, 0.7 to 1.5 times what the same estimator gives on `kitchen`'s dynamic objects (section 2.7): the estimator's own error between cameras is as large as the disagreement it would have to show. One estimator was run, Depth Anything V2 Small, whose training sets hold no people. No normal estimator was run at all.
- **That the shell is where the disagreement is.** No probe located the shell's primitives against the disagreement, in space or in time. Section 2.6 found instead that the notes' two measures count different primitives. The far-5 cm share counts longer-lived primitives (a median duration of 3.9 frames on the near side) and takes 54 % to 68 % of its value from the background, where init points are thin. The under-two-frames share counts short-lived primitives within 5 cm of an init point: 65 % of the primitives on the person's side are under two frames. The median opacity of 0.16 and the lifetime of two frames by which the notes describe the shell are the whole model's.
- **That those measures are peculiar to cameras that disagree.** They were never taken on a scene whose cameras agree, so there is no control. Three facts on record say a control is worth taking, and settle nothing. `kitchen`'s default model is fainter than private scene A's: a mean base opacity of 0.235 with 61 % of its primitives under 0.1 (`docs/prd/questions.md`, the Calibration data row for kitchen at 549o), against 0.360 with a median of 0.160 (section 2.6). On `kitchen` 29 % of the weight lies behind the surface at δ, and on the three scenes' dynamic pixels 44 % to 65 %; but that residual counts a primitive whether or not something hides it, and its δ is a sixth of the notes' 5 cm, so it is not the far-5 cm share (section 2.6). And short-lived primitives on moving content are reported under real, synchronized cameras, by 4DGS-1K for another 4D model (row B7) and by FreeTimeGS++ for this one (section 3.5's closing paragraphs; that statement rests on the authors' reproduction of FreeTimeGS).
- **Three things nobody measured.** Whether one set of corrected cameras brings all the pairs together, which section 2.7 names as the first thing to settle. Any colour difference between the cameras. And the person's outline: it rests on the tools' default rectangle for seven cameras and on a sheet the owner has not yet checked (sections 2.5 and 2.7).

**What only a training test can show.** H2 itself, in three ways.

- **By removal.** If correcting the cameras, or absorbing the disagreement in the loss, lowers the shell's measures, the shell was answering the disagreement (finalists 1 and 2).
- **By H2's own prediction.** If a surface constraint lowers the measures and the renders turn soft, H2 holds against H1 alone. If they stay sharp, H1 holds alone (finalists 3 and 4).
- **By induction, where ground truth exists.** Turning `kitchen`'s cameras by the 0.1° to 0.4° measured here and training the default configuration would show whether such an error alone produces the measures (finalist 1's control, section 4.5).

**The answer.** The premise holds for the cameras' disagreement and for the position of a depth prior made per camera. It is not seen for such a prior's shape. About the shell it says nothing yet: the evidence that the shell is a reconciliation is absent, not thin. It is the notes' inference, and the probes' figures neither support nor contradict it. One figure cuts against its simplest reading, the size of what was measured, and one control is missing, the same measures on cameras that agree. That is why section 4.6 puts checks that need no training before any finalist.

### 4.3 The finalists

**How the 85 rows were cut.** Four rules, in this order; section 4.8's last column gives each row's reason.

1. **The PRD's two bars.** A row that needs rasterization kernels gsplat 1.5.3 lacks (decision 1's third edge), or median depth or the pairwise distortion, which section 2.4 says E6's render cannot supply today, is recorded and is not a finalist. A row whose own estimator fails the bar or is unclear stays eligible only in a form with a passing estimator, which sections 3.3 and 3.4 name for every kind of prior map but scene flow (decision 3).
2. **What the probes found on this scene.** Four kinds of row meet a measured obstacle. Rows that assume cameras agreeing to within about a pixel: the person's matches are 1.84 px apart at the median and the manifest's cameras are off by tenths of a degree (section 2.5). Rows that place a surface through an alignment to triangulated points: the points of two pairs disagree by 4 % of the depth (section 2.7). Rows whose signal would mark the person, in every camera and frame, as the content to leave out (section 3.6, reading 2). And rows that need a camera trusted above the others, which decision 4 says the scene does not have.
3. **What the notes already tried** (section 2.1). Rows that press on the base opacity are the neighbours of `loss.lambda_reg` and `reloc.dead_opacity`, which the notes took from no gain to collapse; and the base opacity does not tell the shell from the surface (medians of 0.12 and 0.18, section 2.6).
4. **Among the rest, what a test can decide.** A finalist has to say beforehand what H1 and H2 each predict for it, and which of section 2.6's two populations it should move. Each finalist below says how thin its evidence is.

**Finalist 1: the cameras corrected once, before training, from background matches** (the variant in row E6; row E15 for the epipolar form of the fit).

- **What it is.** One correction per camera, nine in all, fitted once to the dense matcher's matches on the background over all frames, written into a corrected scene manifest. Training itself is unchanged. Section 2.5 fitted such corrections pair by pair, as turns of 0.10° to 0.41°, and they held from the even frames to the odd.
- **Why it is chosen.** It is the only row that acts on something a probe both measured and removed: the background's residual fell from 0.29 to 2.24 px to 0.28 to 0.60 px. It changes no spec item and needs no estimator, no render output and no prototype code inside training.
- **What it is certain to act on, and what it may not reach.** An epipolar fit, section 2.5's or row E15's, reads the part of a camera's error that lies across the epipolar lines. That is the background's part, and such a fit acts on it and on nothing else: section 2.7 turned the cameras by section 2.5's fits, and the person's figures between the cameras of a pair moved by less than 0.1 % of the depth. The 4 % by which two pairs place the person apart is another kind of error, a turn along the epipolar lines and a scale, which that fit holds at zero. To reach it the fit has to read what section 2.7 read, the agreement in depth of one camera's two partners, on the background; or pairs beyond D-22's, since only five of the nine cameras sit in two of its pairs. And the 4 % may not be the cameras' at all: for `cam3` and `cam6` the relation that suits the person leaves the background 6.8 % and 3.7 % off, which an error of the cameras would not (section 2.7).
- **Against H1 and H2.** H2 predicts lower shell measures on the corrected cameras, by as much as the corrected part feeds the shell. H1 predicts little change, since nothing constrains the surface. If the fit reaches the offset between pairs, the population it should move is the longer-lived primitives off the triangulated points, on the near side. If it reaches the background's part only, little change is expected on the person, and the test says how much a camera error of this size matters by itself.
- **What it does not do.** It does not act on the person's part: after the turns the person's median is still 0.88 to 3.96 px, and in three pairs the turn that suits the background suits the person less (section 2.5). Finalist 2 is there for that part.
- **How thin the evidence is.** Nothing was trained on corrected cameras, no fit was made of all nine cameras as one set, and whether any fit reaches the 4 % is open, as above. The finalist's first step settles the last two without training (section 4.5). The published forms optimize poses inside training: ViDAR's ablation is worth 0.6 dB on its data, and 3R-GS's best practice ends at a rotation error of 0.195°, the size of the error measured here (rows E6, E15).

**Finalist 2: an alignment-tolerant pixel loss** (row E16, AligNeRF's). It is the finalist that is only a loss on today's primitives.

- **What it is.** E7's pixel term in another form: each patch of the render is compared with the best-matching patch of the training image within a shift of up to 8 px, at a penalty per pixel of shift. It reads the render and the training image only, has no parameters, and trusts no camera. With its field off, E7 is as it is; with it on, L_img's L1 changes, which is D-7's choice, and E7's composition and λ_img and λ_ssim, the [P] part, stay (spec E7; section 5.4).
- **Why it is chosen.** It is the only loss on today's primitives that acts on the person's part of the disagreement without a reference camera, a mask or a second model. The residuals section 2.5 left after the turns, 0.9 to 4.0 px at the median and 2.6 to 12.4 px at the 90th percentile, lie within or near its window. Every other answer to the person's part in section 3.6 fails rule 2 or is a far larger change: a deformation per camera needs a reference view (rows E8 to E10), weights from residuals or features would remove the person (rows E3, E4, E12, E19), and the two signals that trust no camera need a second model or two views per iteration (rows E11, E21, E22).
- **Against H1 and H2.** In section 3.6's words, what the shell does with primitives, this loss does by moving the target. H2 predicts that the short-lived primitives near the init points become unnecessary: a lower under-two-frames share on the near side, and fewer superimposed faces in the sheets. H1 predicts no change in where primitives lie. If the measures do not move, either the shell is not a reconciliation, or the disagreement is not a shift of a few pixels that is rigid over a patch.
- **Its risk.** The loss does not say where the surface is. A model that satisfies every camera up to a shift has no reason to prefer one geometry, and only the penalty pulls towards the unshifted target (section 3.6's reading). Softer renders are the expected price, which is why sharpness is in the criteria.
- **How thin the evidence is.** Thin: one scene of one paper, on a NeRF with real cameras, + 0.10 dB on the standard test set and + 0.25 dB on a test set warped onto the model. Nothing on Gaussian primitives, on 4D, or on generated views. Its cost here was not measured.

**Finalist 3: surface terms without a prior, on today's primitives** (rows A1 and A3, with row A5's depth; the formulation section 3.1 reads in PGSR).

- **What it is.** Two arms. First the flattening loss alone, the L1 norm of each primitive's smallest scale, which is only a loss on today's primitives. Then, added to it, depth–normal consistency in the one form section 2.4 can supply today: the direction of the smallest scale as a primitive's normal, composited through Q-226's device, against the normal of the rendered depth map. No estimator and no prior map.
- **Why it is chosen.** It is H1's constraint with nothing else in it, so it separates what a surface term does from what a prior adds. Flattening is the term every planar method read keeps and the precondition of every normal on a 3D Gaussian. Depth–normal consistency is the surface term with the most ablations behind it, and section 2.4 can supply its outputs for today's primitives without a median (cells 2d, 4d and 5d). The rows it is chosen over: the multi-view terms assume agreement within a pixel and harm where views do not match, by PGSR's own ablation (row A6); the co-planarity loss needs a neighbour list per time that its paper does not define (row A2); the depth distortion loss has the smallest effect in its own ablation and made floaters when it was allowed to move the weights (row A4); flat primitives are a second E6 (row A14).
- **Against H1 and H2.** H1 predicts a lower far-5 cm share on the near side and a body that keeps its shape in the side views. H2 predicts one of two outcomes: the measures fall and the renders turn soft, or the shell stays and becomes flat, thin and aligned with its own depth, which is all a single-view term can ask (section 3.1's closing paragraphs). The first arm alone predicts nothing about the shell (row A1: it shapes primitives and does not move them); it is run to see what flattening does to today's spheres before anything reads their normals.
- **How thin the evidence is.** Thin for the form that can be supplied. The five ablations of row A3 are for neighbouring forms: flat primitives, kernel-made normals or median depth. For 3D Gaussians with the smallest-scale normal there are three figures, all on static scenes whose cameras agree: PGSR's single-view term is worth 0.03 of F-score on Tanks and Temples, where its multi-view terms are worth 0.20; Gaussian Opacity Fields measured the smallest-scale normal at 0.40 against 0.46 for its kernel-made normal and 0.37 for no consistency term; TrimGS's term lowers a mesh's Chamfer distance from 1.19 to 0.83 while its point-based figure rises from 1.27 to 1.32, with median depth, which E6's render does not supply. Flattening is isolated once, in VCR-GauS (F-score 0.36 without its scale loss, 0.40 with; row C8), a figure section 3.1 did not have. Two technical risks are known. The smallest scale is a three-way tie for today's spheres, so the consistency term needs flattened primitives or a late start. And E6's depth is one value per primitive, constant across its footprint, which disagrees with a slanted flat primitive's normal; PGSR's plane-distance depth (row A5) answers that with composited constants, in a form section 3.1 read and did not run.

**Finalist 4: a normal prior, on top of finalist 3's flattening** (rows C7 and C8).

- **What it is.** A camera-space normal map per training image, from a pretrained estimator, supervising two things: the normals of the rendered depth map (row C8's regularizer, whose gradient reaches positions) and the composited normals of the primitives (row C7's loss, which turns them). The maps are files written before training; nothing enters the package.
- **Why it is chosen.** It is H1 as the owner worded it, with the one kind of prior that section 2.7's finding does not reach: a normal has no scale, no shift and no anchor, so the 4 % between pairs and the misfit of one scale and shift per image do not enter it. The depth priors it is chosen over either pass through an alignment and inherit both (rows C1, C12, the absolute term of C5), or are taken within one image and gain nothing once views are many: DNGaussian's gain is 2.7 dB with 3 views and none with 9 (row C2; rows C3 and C4 are of its kind), and private scene A trains on eight or nine cameras.
- **The estimator.** MoGe-2's `Ruicheng/moge-2-vitl-normal` passes the bar and works on general scenes, so one estimator serves private scene A and openftgs-synth. DAViD's `normal-model-vitl16_384.onnx` passes too and is made for people; it is meaningful on the person only, and openftgs-synth's scenes give it no floor (section 3.3; section 2.7).
- **Against H1 and H2.** H1 predicts what it predicts for finalist 3, and more of it. H2 predicts that each camera's normals describe that camera's version of the person, so that the loss settles between them and smooths (section 3.4's reading in row C7). Like every loss taken within one image it shapes a surface and cannot place it (section 3.4's closing paragraphs): alone it cannot bring a layer of primitives back onto the body.
- **How thin the evidence is.** Thin, and none of it with an estimator that passes. Row C7's loss took a mesh's F-score from 56.3 to 60.2 on one indoor scene, with Omnidata's normals, which fail the bar. In row C8's ablation on Tanks and Temples the F-score is 0.40 in full, 0.30 without the regularizer, 0.35 when the depth is each primitive's centre depth, which is what E6 supplies, and 0.36 without its confidence weight; its normals there are DSINE's, which fail. Nothing on people, on 4D or on cameras that disagree. No probe ran any normal estimator on private scene A, so this finalist's first step is section 2.7's measurement repeated for normals, without training.

**What the four cover, and what they leave.** Finalists 1 and 2 act on the disagreement, the cameras' part and the person's; finalists 3 and 4 act on the surface. H2's recommendation, a surface constraint paired with a way to absorb the disagreement, is then a combination of one from each side, which is tested only after each has been seen alone. Nothing in the shortlist acts on the base opacity, on colour, on velocities or on orientation that changes with time, and no finalist needs a mask, a crop or a reference camera.

**Flagged by a survey and not proposed.** Every technique a survey's closing paragraphs flagged, with the reason it is not a finalist and what would bring it back.

| Technique (rows) | Why it is not a finalist | What would bring it back |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Known masks left out of the loss (E13) | Screened out as a test of the shell: the shell's measures are the same with `cam0` in training and out of it (section 4.2). It stays the smallest spec change for a defect that is measured | It remains a candidate recommendation for section 7, without a training test of the shell |
| Other values of `loss.lambda_perc` and `loss.perc_net` (E1, E2); a perceptual term alone on the person's crop (E18) | The first are values of existing fields and need no review; the notes tried one. The second's stated failure is views that disagree in more than texture, which is what section 2.5 measured | The owner can run the first at any time. The second, if finalist 2 shows that tolerating shifts helps and costs sharpness |
| A colour correction per camera (E27, E26, E14) | It answers colour, which nobody measured, and decision 4 says the cameras share their colour temperature | A measured colour difference between cameras (item 15) |
| Two models' disagreement, gradient conflict per primitive (E11, E22, E21) | A second model, or two views per iteration against D-26; no table isolates the first | The check on two existing checkpoints that differ by seed alone (item 15): do two models disagree where the shell is? |
| A deformation per camera (E8 to E10); primitives per view (E25) | The largest changes of family E. The first needs a reference camera, which the scene lacks; both change what a test view's score means | The owner naming a reference camera (item 11), or finalist 2 showing that the person's part matters and a loss cannot absorb it |
| Pose optimization inside training (E6 as published, E15) | Finalist 1's trained form. Joint optimization blurs a scene part of which differs between views, and the best practice read ends at the size of the error measured | Finalist 1's check showing that a one-off fit cannot reconcile the pairs |
| Re-alignment of the training images (E17) | It alters the data, and it is the costly form of finalist 2 by its own paper | Finalist 2 helping while its penalty proves too weak an anchor |
| The constant opacity decay (B5); 4C4D's learned decay (B1) | The steadiest evidence with few cameras, but it presses on the base opacity (rule 3), and its argument assumes cameras that agree: under H2 it removes first what one camera alone supports | First reserve on the opacity side, if finalists 1 and 2 remove the disagreement and faint primitives remain |
| The loss that lengthens lifespans (B9) | The only term whose gradient reaches the duration, with no ablation and no published weight. Its tried relatives were mixed: a stride-4 init gained 0.2 to 0.6 dB, a faster duration rate 0.17 dB and looked softer | As a companion to finalist 2, if tolerating shifts leaves durations short |
| The entropy loss on opacity (B6); the effective rank (B10); the anisotropy bound (B15) | The entropy loss's evidence points both ways and it sits beside the settings the notes took to collapse. The effective rank's flattening part is finalist 3's. The anisotropy bound limits the ratio a flattening loss drives up | None proposed |
| Pruning of short-lived primitives (B7, B13) | If H2 holds they remove the means of reconciliation and not its cause; B13's measure as published sits behind a mask whose estimator fails | Finalist 2 lowering the need for short-lived primitives without removing them |
| DNGaussian's hard depth (C2); the depth-inconsistency mask (C5) | Aimed at primitives that turn faint instead of moving, but both supervise with depth priors, whose gains vanish as views are added, and C5's absolute term passes through an alignment | Finalist 4 showing that a prior helps: C5's mask reads only the model's own renders and could gate it |
| Initialization from prior maps (C10 to C15), with the owner's seed MotionCrafter (C15) | It can be tried against today's code for positions and velocities, and would cover the 73 % of the person's pixels without a match; but it lifts per-camera depth through an alignment and inherits the 4 %. MotionCrafter fails the bar, and no estimator of scene flow passes | After finalist 1: on cameras that agree, a denser start is the next cheap thing to try. Oriented starts (A10, C10) if finalist 3's orientations never settle |
| The flow loss (C9); agreement of velocity between neighbours (D8) | Nothing measured implicates the velocities: starting them at zero left the notes' measures where they were (section 2.6, `stride4` against `stride4_zero_velocity`). Rigidity over nearest neighbours lowered PSNR by 0.6 to 2 dB in the one controlled comparison (row D7) | None proposed |
| 4DSurf's agreement of motion along the normal with the change of depth (D4) | The only term of family D isolated on a geometry metric, on flat primitives with a canonical shape; it needs normals and depth at two times a step | Finalist 3 leaving a surface that is right in each frame and unstable between frames |
| A persistence gate, a lifespan radius, a rotation in time (D2, D3, B8, D5) | Each changes a [P] quantity. The gate's gain is inside its paper's run-to-run spread, and no row reports what turning a primitive buys in geometry | A finalist showing that constant orientation or E4's profile is what limits it |
| Flat primitives (A14) | Eligible under the recommended answer to item 6, and not proposed: a second E6, new lines in U-7, U-8 and U-12, and no route but a spec revision | Finalist 3 failing for a reason that is the 3D Gaussian's, such as its depth |

### 4.4 The proposed criteria

The PRD's starting proposal ("Training tests") has two halves. On private scene A, where no camera is ground truth, better means lower shell measures and novel-view sheets the owner judges better, and test-view scores only guard against harm. On openftgs-synth, the geometry residual and the error of expected depth are no worse with the technique on than off, and E16's metrics stay within the recorded run-to-run spread. The probes change it in ten places.

| The PRD's line | What was found | Proposed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| "Lower shell measures" | The two measures count different primitives; over 30 runs their rank correlation is 0.44 (section 2.6) | Reported apart and never combined. Each finalist names beforehand the one it should move (section 4.3) |
| The far-5 cm share | It depends on the init points file it is taken against, by up to 0.02 between files of one size and 0.03 to 0.04 against a denser one, and 54 % to 68 % of it is background (section 2.6). Finalist 1's arms have init points of their own | Its near-side part, against one fixed reference file for every arm of a comparison. For finalist 1, against two references triangulated from the same matches, one with the manifest's cameras and one with the corrected ones; it counts as lower only against both |
| The under-two-frames share | Of the near side's primitives 65 % are under two frames, of the far side's 16 % (section 2.6) | Its near-side part, with the whole model's beside it |
| The noise on those measures | Between seeds the far-5 cm share differs by 0.001 to 0.002 in three pairs of runs and 0.010 in the fourth; the under-two-frames share by 0.002 to 0.004; the near-side part by 0.003 in the one pair measured (section 2.6) | Two seeds per arm. A measure has moved when both of the arm's seeds lie beyond both of the baseline's and the means differ by more than 0.01 |
| "Sheets the owner judges better" | Seven checkpoints have a sheet from one fixed request, and nobody has looked at one. Two of them differ by seed alone (section 2.6). The PRD's proposal has no measure of blur, which is what H2 predicts | The same request, baseline and arm side by side with their labels hidden, after the owner has seen how much two seeds differ. Beside the sheets, two numbers: the notes' sharpness ratio on the test view's crop, which section 2.6 names and does not define, and the sheet's tile coverage (the share of a tile's pixels with alpha ≥ 0.5), against collapse |
| Test-view scores "no worse than the notes' noise between seeds" | The noise is ± 0.01 dB on the centre test view. Under decision 4 the score measures agreement with one generated camera, so a technique that stops fitting that camera's version of the person can lower it without harm, and ± 0.01 dB would flag any change at all. For finalist 1 the held-out camera's pose changes too (section 3.6, reading 3) | Reported for every run. A fall beyond the seed noise is weighed with the sheets and is not a failure by itself; a fall of more than 0.3 dB, all that the notes' best configuration recovered (section 2.1), counts as harm. Finalist 1's models are scored at both poses of the held-out camera, the manifest's and the corrected one |
| "Placeholder pixels left out" (Q-233) | They are `cam0`'s only, 0.445 % of its crop (section 2.5), and the owner has not checked the rule's sheet | Applied only where `cam0` is the test view, by section 2.5's rule, once the owner has checked it (item 12) |
| On openftgs-synth, "a geometry residual no worse" | The residual's shares are steady between trainings of one configuration: within 0.003 on `kitchen` and 0.004 on `studio`. Most of the weight off the surface is behind it (section 2.6) | A share is worse, or better, when it differs from the off arm's by more than 0.005: in front and behind at δ, on all pixels and on dynamic pixels |
| "An error of expected depth no worse" | Trainings of one configuration differ by up to 47 % in its median (section 2.6) | Reported with each seed's value. A criterion only where the three-seed ranges of two arms do not overlap |
| "E16's metrics within the run-to-run spread that `docs/prd/validation-runs.md` records" | Two records exist, an order of magnitude apart. One repeat of `kitchen`'s default run: 0.05 dB of PSNR and 0.001 of SSIM₁ (step 5, trained with LPIPS). Three seeds per configuration in `docs/research/perceptual-alternatives.md`, section 6.6: a standard deviation of 0.55 to 1.54 dB of PSNR on `cam00`, with ꟻLIP's three seeds at 28.38, 27.01 and 26.58 dB, and of at most 0.0023 of SSIM₁. At 549o each scene has one training | Three seeds per arm on `kitchen`. The guard is SSIM₁: the arm's mean not below the off arm's by more than 0.002, the margin the owner set in #374. PSNR, kept-pixel PSNR and ꟻLIP are reported per seed, and flagged when all three of the arm's values lie below all three of the off arm's. On `studio`, one seed: dynamic-region PSNR, flagged when lower by more than 0.1 dB (three trainings before 549o lie within 0.02 dB) |

**How a result would be read.** On private scene A a finalist has done what it was chosen for when the measure it named has moved, the owner judges its sheets no worse, the sharpness ratio has not fallen beyond what two seeds give, and no test-view score has fallen by more than 0.3 dB. A measure that moves while the renders turn soft is recorded as the trade H2 predicts, and is not a pass. On openftgs-synth a finalist does no harm when no residual share is worse and SSIM₁ holds; a surface finalist is expected to do better there, by a lower share behind the surface.

**What these criteria rest on, and where that is thin.** The thresholds on private scene A rest on four pairs of runs that differ by seed, and for the near-side part on one. The sharpness ratio's spread between seeds is not in section 2.6 and would be taken from the baseline's two seeds. The near side is the primitives nearer than 2.0 along the centre camera's axis, where the init points say the person is; no image was seen to confirm it. And the notes' measures have never been taken on openftgs-synth, so a criterion that uses them there waits for item 15's first check. The statistics, if the owner wants tests and not thresholds, would be declared before any result, as #374's were; three seeds a side gave little power there.

### 4.5 The route for each finalist, and its GPU hours

**What the three routes are worth here.** `Trainer.step` calls `render` and `total_loss` by their names in `openftgs.train`, and a `Callback` runs only after a step is complete, so the public API has no place where a term can be added before the backward pass (`src/openftgs/train.py` at 93eb58e).

- **A scratch script outside the package** drives `Trainer` and, at run time, puts wrappers in place of those two names: one adds the new terms to `total_loss`, the other hands it the camera that Q-226's device needs. No file of the package changes and nothing is committed. It has to prove itself first. With every added weight at 0, one run must reproduce a plain `ftgs train` run within the tolerance #395's parity check met for the package: the losses over the first 1,000 iterations equal to four figures, N at iteration 500 within 0.01 %, and the cap reached at the same iteration (`docs/prd/questions.md`, the Calibration data for kitchen at 549o). A match bit for bit is not to be had, since gsplat's backward is not bit-repeatable (Q-220).
- **A throwaway uncommitted worktree** edits `Trainer.step` and `total_loss` in place. The parity run is the same. Its risk is that an edit never to be committed sits beside real code, and `PYTHONPATH` decides which of the two a command runs.
- **Waiting for the spec revision** tests the technique behind its field. Its figures are the adopted code's, and it asks the owner for a spec revision before there is any evidence.

A prototype's figures are a prototype's, whatever the route. Through the device a depth or normal output costs half an iteration to one iteration more, where a channel of the colour pass would cost 2 % to 13 % (section 2.4), so no test here measures the training cost of an adopted technique. And a loss on the device's render does not enter E10's g unless the prototype adds it (section 2.4): the tests below leave it out, so growth and relocation keep reading the rendering loss alone.

**The recommended route, per finalist.**

| Finalist | Route | First step, without training | Why this route |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Cameras corrected once | None of the three: a script writes a corrected scene manifest, then `ftgs init roma` and `ftgs train` run unchanged | Fit one correction per camera on the background, to the epipolar residuals of all seven pairs and to the agreement in depth of one camera's two partners, and repeat sections 2.5's and 2.7's measures with it on held-out frames. Two outcomes. If the background's residual falls for every pair and the person's 4.0 % between pairs falls towards the 1.0 % to 1.6 % that section 2.7's fitted relation leaves, the finalist tests the cameras' whole part. If only the background's residual falls, it tests the background's part alone, and the owner decides whether that is worth its runs | The package is untouched and the results are the package's own. A correction that is kept becomes the manifest every later arm trains on |
| 2. Alignment-tolerant loss | A scratch script | Time the patch search on one training image, which no section measured | It reads the render and the training image only, so the wrapper of `total_loss` is all it needs. Its form is still to be found (the window, the penalty, whether SSIM and ꟻLIP shift with the pixel term), so a spec revision would come too early |
| 3. Surface terms without a prior | A scratch script on Q-226's device | Check on one flat, slanted primitive that row A5's plane-distance depth, composited through the device with its signed distance coded as the normal is, returns the plane's depth: section 3.1 read that form and did not run it | Section 2.4 measured the device's gradients on both render backends within U-12's bounds. The `RenderOutput` fields the technique would end in are a §2.1 change, which should wait for the result |
| 4. Normal prior | A scratch script on the device, with the prior maps as files from a scratch environment | Section 2.7's measurement repeated for normals: the estimator's normals turned into the world by the manifest's rotations and compared between the cameras of a pair on the person, with `kitchen` for the floor | As finalist 3. Reading maps from files puts no estimator in the package and leaves §2.2 and §2.3 to the spec revision |

**The runs counted.** Every arm, the baseline included, gets the same two sets, at 30,000 iterations of the default training configuration.

- **On private scene A, three runs:** the copy that holds the centre camera out, at seeds 0 and 1, and the copy that holds out the camera left of it, at seed 0. These are the notes' two copies (section 2.6), from a stride-4 init as the notes recommend. Each run is followed by its shell measures and its sheet.
- **On openftgs-synth, four runs:** `kitchen` at seeds 0, 1 and 2 and `studio` at seed 0 (the scenes of Q-220's runs), each followed by `scripts/gt_depth.py` and the kept-pixel PSNR. `kitchen` gives the seeds, and `studio` a dynamic subject with exact masks. Finalist 1 has no arm there, since openftgs-synth's cameras are exact; its optional control is counted apart.

**The hours,** on one RTX 4090, one run at a time.

| What | Runs | Cost against a default run | GPU hours |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The baseline | The two sets by plain `ftgs train`, and two parity runs of the script with every added weight at 0, one on private scene A and one on `kitchen` | 1 | 2.5 |
| Finalist 1 | The first step; two inits on the corrected manifest; the three runs on private scene A | 1 | 2 |
| Finalist 1's control, optional | `kitchen` with its cameras turned by 0.1° to 0.4°, and the same after the correction: two inits, and two seeds each | 1 | 2 more |
| Finalist 2 | One arm at the paper's window and penalty, on both sets | 1 to 2, not measured | 2 to 4 |
| Finalist 3 | Two arms on both sets: flattening alone, then with depth–normal consistency | 1, then 2 (two passes of the device) | 6 |
| Finalist 4 | The prior maps and the first step; one arm on both sets with MoGe-2's normals | 2 (two passes of the device) | 4.5 |
| Finalist 4's second estimator, optional | One arm on private scene A with DAViD's normals | 2 | 2 more |
| If a run on private scene A takes twice the notes' median | Its 19 runs above | 2 | 7.5 to 8.5 more |

All four with the baseline come to 17 to 19 GPU hours, and to 25 to 27 if a run on private scene A takes twice the notes' median. The two options add 4 to 6. Finalists 1 and 2 with the baseline come to 6.5 to 8.5, or 9.5 to 12.5.

**The assumptions.**

- **A run on private scene A takes 18 minutes,** the median of the notes' 40 training runs, which took 2.4 to 73 minutes each (the notes' results file, row 513). Those runs mix caps and settings, which is why the table gives the hours at twice that too.
- **A run on openftgs-synth takes what M17's did at 549o:** 14 min 22 s on `kitchen` and 18 min 55 s on `studio` (`docs/prd/validation-runs.md`, steps 35 and 37), with a minute and a half of diagnostics after each (section 2.6's commands). A `kitchen` init takes 30 minutes (step 1).
- **A pass of the device adds half an iteration:** 14.8 ms against an iteration of 25.45 to 30.9 ms on the trained `kitchen` model (section 2.4). The same share is assumed on private scene A, where it was not measured.
- **One arm per finalist, at its paper's settings.** A second value of a weight or a window is one more arm at the same hours. No arm combines two finalists: a combination of one from each side follows the single results and is not counted.
- **Minutes are rounded in.** The inits on private scene A, the sheets, the prior maps (MoGe-2 states 60 ms an image, and the three scenes hold about 8,200 training images) and the first steps are counted as an hour at most each, on the figures of sections 2.5 and 2.7, whose probes used 73 and 67 minutes of GPU.

**The order proposed.** The checks of item 15; then finalist 1's first step and, if the owner keeps it after that, its runs; then finalist 2, on the corrected cameras if the correction was kept; then finalist 3; then finalist 4 on top of it. Each result goes to the owner before the next step starts, so the owner can stop after any of them.

### 4.7 The owner's answers at review 1

Review 1 took place on 2026-11-16, in session, between the owner and the orchestrating session, on the material of sections 4.1 to 4.6. The answers, as the owner gave them; the letters and numbers are the lines of section 4.6.

- **F1 to F4, the finalists.** All four are kept: the cameras corrected once before training, the alignment-tolerant pixel loss, the surface terms without a prior, and the normal prior on top of them. Keeping finalist 1 goes with the yes to item 11c below. **Answered** (2026-11-16).
- **C, the criteria.** Section 4.4's are accepted as drafted, the 0.3 dB guard on a test-view score and the sheets judged with their labels hidden included. **Confirmed** (2026-11-16).
- **R, H and K: the routes, the hours and the control on `kitchen`.** No tests in this PRD. Nothing trains under PRD #1: the document stops at the test plan (section 5), which records the finalists and how they would be tried, and the recommendation (section 7) rests on the evidence from papers and from the probes. Section 4.5's routes, hours and control stand as that plan's proposal and clear no run. **Answered** (2026-11-16).
- **Items 5 to 18.** All take their recommended answers. **Confirmed** (2026-11-16), which gives:
  - 5: forming median depth or the pairwise distortion from gsplat 1.5.3's pair list does not count as "gsplat 1.5.3 can supply it".
  - 6: flat-primitive techniques are eligible as finalists; none is proposed.
  - 7: a depth a paper computes in its own kernels counts as suppliable where it composites as channels, after a numeric check.
  - 8: the "unclear" weights rows are screened out, and the "fails as stated" rows keep failing.
  - 9: three class labels are added for this document only: an initialization; trained beside the primitives; a preprocessing.
  - 11a: no camera is the reference. 11b: finalist 1 is scored at both poses of the held-out camera and judged by the shell's measures and the sheets. 11c: a one-off correction of the manifest's cameras is in scope, as preprocessing.
  - 13: the tools' default rectangle. 16: section 3.3 keeps no nine-column table. 17: the two "default" marks of the opening are confirmed (decision 1's core; the PRD's extension of the privacy instruction). 18: the document's length is tightened at the step-7 review.
- **Items 10, 14 and 15, and what does not run in this PRD.** Asked the same day which of the work that trains nothing should run before the test plan is written (the six checks of item 15, the three further reads of item 10, the fourth nine-camera model of item 14), the owner answered: none, straight to the test plan. So no further check, read or measurement is made under PRD #1. The three reads (FreeTimeGS++ in full, SparseCam4D, SSA-3DGS), the fourth nine-camera model, which may be opened, and the six checks in section 4.6's order are written into the test plan as the first work of a later PRD. **Answered** (2026-11-16).
- **Item 12, the owner's check of the placeholder-pixel sheets.** Pending at review 1; made later the same day, 2026-11-16, in session, and recorded in section 2.5: the detections are the magenta artifacts, the loose rule's additions belong to them, the pink population is not an artifact, and the default rectangle crops the person a little on some images. **Answered** (2026-11-16).

What follows for the later sections: section 5 (ticket #14) plans for the four finalists and opens with the work that trains nothing; section 6 (ticket #15) records that no finalist was cleared to train under this PRD; and a finalist stays a proposal to test, so section 7's recommendation cannot rest on a test of any of them.

### 4.8 The consolidated screening table

One row per technique of the five families, 85 in all: 17 from section 3.1, 15 from section 3.2, 15 from section 3.4, 11 from section 3.5 and 27 from section 3.6. Section 3.3 screens estimators, not techniques, and its rows are in section 4.9. A row's letter is its family and its number its place in that family's table, which holds the detail and the source-log row behind every cell; nothing is cited afresh here. A cell is a phrase, and where it and the family's cell differ in wording, the family's cell is the record.

**The columns.**

- **Class:** the integration class, in short. "Loss" is a loss on today's primitives; "loss + outputs" a loss that needs new render outputs; "growth" a change to growth, relocation or split; "quantity" a change to a quantity or to motion; "kernels" new rasterization kernels. Three more labels follow the recommended answer to item 9: "initialization"; "beside the primitives", for parameters or a model trained beside them (per camera, per image, a second model, the optimizer's step); and "preprocessing", of the data or of the scene manifest. Where a family gave a nearest class, it stands in brackets.
- **Render backends:** what the technique needs from them, and whether section 2.4 says it can be supplied today, with the cells of that section's table. A "d" cell is Q-226's device and an "a" cell a channel of the colour pass.
- **Estimator:** the estimator it needs and its verdict against the bar, from section 4.9; "none" means no pretrained network.
- **Cameras:** whether it assumes cameras whose images agree.
- **Evidence** and **Cost:** a phrase each. The figures are the papers', as the family rows cite them; an arrow reads "without → with".
- **Finalist?:** the finalist it belongs to, or why it is none, by the rules of section 4.3.

| Row | Technique | Class | Render backends | Estimator | Cameras | Evidence | Cost | Finalist? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A1 | Flattening loss on the smallest scale (PGSR) | loss | nothing | none | nothing between cameras; cannot tell a flat primitive on the surface from one off it | not isolated in section 3.1's reads; in VCR-GauS, F-score 0.36 without its scale loss and 0.40 with (row C8) | negligible | **Finalist 3,** first arm |
| A2 | Co-planarity loss between neighbouring primitives (GeoGaussian) | loss | nothing; it reads `visible` | none | nothing between cameras: a smoothness prior | not isolated | a neighbour search, not timed | No: needs a neighbour list per time, which its paper does not define |
| A3 | Depth–normal consistency loss (2DGS; forms in Gaussian Surfels, PGSR, Gaussian Opacity Fields, RaDe-GS, TrimGS) | loss + outputs | a composited normal and the depth map's normal: **yes** on expected depth (2d, 4d, 5d). The forms on median depth: **no** (3a, 3d) | none | single view; one opaque surface per pixel | isolated in five papers on static scenes, in neighbouring forms (Chamfer distance 1.24 → 0.83 in 2DGS); thin for the form supplied (section 4.3) | 10 to 13 % as channels; two passes of the device, about one iteration more | **Finalist 3,** second arm |
| A4 | Depth distortion loss (2DGS; Gaussian Opacity Fields; RaDe-GS) | loss + outputs | the squared form: **yes** (7d). The absolute form: **no** (6a, 6d) | none | single view; one opaque surface per ray | 0.88 → 0.83, the smaller of 2DGS's two terms; made floaters where it moved the weights | one pass of the device | No: the smallest effect of the surface terms; a possible second term of finalist 3 |
| A5 | Plane-distance depth (PGSR) | loss + outputs: it is an output | a composited normal and one more composited scalar: **yes** by section 3.1's reading (4d, 8d), not run | none | single view | F-score 0.38 without it, 0.52 with, in PGSR | as A3 | Part of finalist 3: the depth its consistency term may read |
| A6 | Multi-view geometric and photometric consistency (PGSR) | loss + outputs, with a second render per iteration | A5's outputs for two cameras: yes | none | **assumes agreement:** its weight is 0 from 1 px of error | the largest effect in PGSR (0.32 → 0.52); it harms where views do not match (0.28) | about one more colour pass | No: rule 2 |
| A7 | Regularization toward a distance function of the primitives' density (SuGaR) | loss + outputs, with a neighbour search | a depth map: yes on expected depth (2d) | none | one opaque surface that every camera sees alike | not isolated in numbers | 15 to 45 minutes a scene, several times 3DGS's | No: not isolated, and slow |
| A8 | Relative depth loss on median depth against multi-view stereo depth, over three views (Kim et al.) | loss + outputs, with a depth map from outside and three renders | median depth: **no** (3a, 3d); mean depth is clearly worse by its paper | none: classical stereo | trusts stereo depth, which exists where cameras agree | 0.63 on DTU for the whole method; its parts in figures | 58 minutes against 18 | No: rule 1 (median depth), rule 2 |
| A9 | Mutual supervision between PatchMatch and the rendered depth (CoMVS-GS) | loss + outputs, with an external program inside training | A5's outputs: yes | none; OpenMVS, whose license was not read | supervises only where three views agree | 0.579 → 0.512 m on one scene | not stated | No: rule 2 |
| A10 | Oriented, pre-flattened initialization from points with normals (GeoGaussian; CoMVS-GS) | initialization (family: none of the five; nearest, a quantity) | nothing | none | orients primitives where init points exist, which is where the cameras agree within 2 px | not isolated from the density of the points | once, at the start | No: needs a key for orientation in §2.2; a remedy if finalist 3's orientations never settle |
| A11 | Clone and split in the tangent plane (GeoGaussian) | growth | nothing | none | not about cameras | not isolated | not timed | No: not isolated; needs thin primitives first |
| A12 | Trimming by contribution, with a split of large primitives (TrimGS) | growth | a statistic per primitive, for which section 2.4 has no cell; a changed kernel in its paper | none | a primitive that explains one camera contributes fully to it and is kept (section 3.1's reading) | 1.35 → 1.22 on DTU, fine-tuning | a pass over all training images every 1,000 iterations | No: it keeps what one camera alone supports |
| A13 | Growth by the sum of per-pixel gradient norms (Gaussian Opacity Fields; AbsGS) | growth | gsplat 1.5.3 has `absgrad`; whether it is the paper's statistic was not checked | none | would send growth to where the images disagree (section 3.1's reading) | F-score 0.44 → 0.46; LPIPS 0.234 → 0.199 on 3DGS | none stated | No: it moves growth toward the disagreement |
| A14 | Flat disk primitive with a ray–splat intersection (2DGS) | quantity (its eligibility is item 6) | `rasterization_2dgs` supplies it (column c), with median depth, normals and distortion; the reference backend would need a second E6 | none | a change of primitive, not of what is asked of the cameras | 0.80 against 1.96 for 3DGS on DTU; training-view PSNR 34.52 against 35.76 | 3DGS's time; its backward synchronizes with the host | No: eligible under item 6 and not proposed (section 4.3) |
| A15 | Surfel with the third scale at zero and a depth slope per pixel (Gaussian Surfels) | quantity, with kernels for its depth (item 7) | its slope term perhaps as three composited channels (8a): section 3.1's algebra, not run | its normal prior reads Omnidata: **fails** | needs foreground masks, which §2.2 refuses on training images | 0.882 on DTU, with an ablation of each term | 6.67 minutes against 5.19 | No: rule 1 as published |

Under item 7, a third depth finalist 3 could read |

**Techniques that two families screened, reconciled.**

- **SharpTimeGS.** Sections 3.1, 3.2 and 3.5 read the same paper, under three source-log rows, and agree: on its class, on its ablation (26.76 → 27.36 dB without and with the radius) and that nothing isolates its lifespan loss. Row D3 is the whole method; rows B8 and B9 are the two parts a field could hold; its PGSR-style terms are in rows A1 and A3.
- **MonoFusion.** Row C12 is its depth alignment and row D11 the whole method. Its masks are SAM 2's, from a user prompt, by section 3.4's read (row 728), which passes; the parts section 3.5 read do not name the model, and section 3.5 now points to section 3.4's read (step-7 review). No verdict here depends on it, since both rows are screened out on other grounds.
- **GC-4DGS.** Rows C4 and C6 only; sections 3.5 and 3.6 name it and hand it to section 3.4.
- **TrimGS.** Its trimming is row A12, and its consistency term is one of row A3's forms. Section 3.2 names it and has no row for it.
- **FreeTimeGS++.** Row D2 is its gate and row E27 its colour correction, with the same figures in section 3.5's hand-over and in section 3.6's row. Its third technique, an initial velocity from optical flow in place of E15, has no row: section 3.5 handed it to section 3.4, which could not read the paper under the interim rule. Its estimator, UFM, fails (section 4.9).
- **4D-Rotor GS.** Row B6 is its entropy loss and row D8 its 4D consistency loss, from one table of the paper; the two sections' figures agree.
- **SpotLessSplats.** Row B13 is its pruning and row E3 its mask; as published, row B13's measure is taken behind row E3's mask.
- **Three more papers with a row in two families.** Dynamic 3D Gaussians: row D9 the method, row E26 its colour correction. AniGS: row E9 its deformation, row B15 the regularizer it borrows, and its normal loss is cited in row C7. 4C4D: row B1, and section 3.5 names it for its four cameras.
- **The flattening loss.** Row A1. Its one ablation is in section 3.4's row on VCR-GauS, an F-score of 0.36 without the scale loss against 0.40 with it; section 3.1 found none in the papers it read, and points to that row (corrected at the step-7 review). DN-Splatter, VCR-GauS and SharpTimeGS each add the term; the effective-rank term of row B10 contains it, and the anisotropy bound of row B15 works against it.
- **The class of an initialization, and of parameters trained beside the primitives.** The families word it differently: "none of the five; nearest, a quantity" in section 3.1, "none of the five: an initialization" in section 3.4, "nearest class: a loss on today's primitives" in section 3.6. Item 9 settles the wording, and no row's eligibility depends on it.

**Named by one family for another, and screened by none.** The five surveys ran at once, and a name handed to a family that had reached its cap got no row. None is a finalist, since none was read in full.

- **For section 3.6:** SparseCam4D (arXiv 2603.26481), which by its abstract models generated observations that are inconsistent with a distortion field over space and time (from section 3.4); and SSA-3DGS (arXiv 2607.05598), a learnable 2D overlay for artifacts fixed to one camera's image plane, the placeholder pixels' case (from section 3.2). Item 10 recommends reading both.
- **For section 3.5:** USplat4D (arXiv 2510.12768), an uncertainty per Gaussian from how often it is observed (from sections 3.4 and 3.6).
- **For section 3.4:** the initial velocity from flow of FreeTimeGS++ (from section 3.5); a depth term of SPIn-NeRF, which section 3.6's read records without its form (row 811) and section 3.4 did not find in the abstract; the depth-ordering loss of Sparse Dynamic Cameras (from section 3.5), known by its abstract; and the normal prior of Gaussian Surfels (from section 3.1), whose kind is row C7 and whose estimator, Omnidata, fails.
- **For section 3.1:** PCAsplat and ARGS (from section 3.2), and MVGSR's multi-view consistency loss (from section 3.6), known by its abstract.

Each family's section also lists the techniques that passed its abstract screen and were not read for want of reads; they have no row there and none here.

### 4.9 The consolidated estimators table

Every estimator the survey names, with its verdict against the bar: the 68 rows of section 3.3 and the 15 rows of the families' own tables (one in section 3.2, four in section 3.4, three in section 3.5, seven in section 3.6; section 3.1 has none). Rows of one estimator that share a verdict are put together, and the third column says how many rows of the source table a line stands for. The publisher's statement, its address, the commit and the date read are in the source table's row, and so is the training data; a verdict holds for that commit and date and is checked again before anything is adopted. On every row, prior maps the user supplies as files would do in the estimator's place. "Named by" gives the screening rows of section 4.8 whose papers use the estimator; "a substitute" marks one no paper read uses, which a family named as a passing replacement.
