**The counts.** The 57 lines above stand for 83 rows. Section 3.3's 68: 18 pass, 39 fail, 7 of them "as stated", and 11 are unclear. The families' 15: 3 pass (SEA-RAFT, and DINOv2 in two tables), 8 fail, 1 of them as stated, and 4 are unclear. No two tables disagree about an estimator: DINOv2 is the only one screened twice, and passes both times.

**What passes, by kind.** Monocular depth: Depth Anything V2 Small with its metric fine-tunes, Intel's versions of DPT, Marigold V2. Video depth: Video Depth Anything Small, FlashDepth-S. Normals: Marigold V2's checkpoint, MoGe-2's `-normal` files and MoGe-3, and DAViD's normal models for people. Point maps: MapAnything's Apache variant, MoGe's family, four files of Depth Anything 3. Flow and tracks: TAPIR's family, AllTracker, SEA-RAFT's file. Masks: SAM, SAM 2, DAViD's soft foreground. Scene flow: nothing, which is why the owner's seed MotionCrafter has no form that passes (row C15).

**What the unclear rows would add, if the owner let them pass** (item 8). For normals, Metric3D v2 and GeoWizard; for depth, the original files of Depth Anything V1, MiDaS and ZoeDepth, which the passing files already cover; for flow, RAFT and WAFT; for masks, BackgroundMattingV2 and Track Anything; for stereo depth, MVSFormer. No finalist needs any of them.

**The estimators a finalist would use.** Only finalist 4 uses one: MoGe-2's `Ruicheng/moge-2-vitl-normal` first, and DAViD's `normal-model-vitl16_384.onnx` as the estimator made for people. Both pass on their publishers' statements as section 3.3 read them on 2026-11-14. §2.3 lists neither, and a test reads their maps from files, so nothing is asked of §2.3 at review 1.

## 5. The test plan

*Ticket #14's section.* For each finalist the owner kept at review 1, before anything trains: the spec items it would touch, by identifier, and how; the fields of the training configuration it would add, with their defaults, and whether the default leaves E1 to E17 and the §3 tests untouched; the prior maps it needs and how they would reach training; the runs, with the criteria agreed at review 1. A sketched signature or field is a proposal for the owner's spec revision, not a Q-n candidate.

Written on 2026-11-16, after review 1. **This is the plan a later PRD would run, not a record of runs.** The owner's answer at review 1 was that nothing trains under PRD #1, and that the checks, reads and measurements that train nothing do not run under it either (section 4.7). So the section says, for the four finalists the owner kept, what each would touch and how it would be tried, and it opens with the work that would come first. It adds no source-log row.

**How a sketch is marked.** Every field, signature, key or command below is a proposal for the owner's spec revision. None is a Q-n candidate, none is code, and no spec item changes here: adopting a technique is the owner's spec change (section 1, "Out of scope"). Where a sketch would change a signature that §2.1 lists (`RenderOutput`, `render`, `LossConfig`, `total_loss`, the CLI block), or public API that a Q-n row records (`SceneManifest`, Q-64), its table says so in those words. The names are placeholders the revision would choose.

**What the spec's tags allow** (§0, D-72). What the finalists change is tagged D-n: finalist 2 changes D-7's choice of L1 as E7's image loss, and finalists 3 and 4 add terms to E9, whose composition is D-37's. The one [P] item in question is E10's g, which reads E9's whole L: section 5.5 leaves open whether the new terms should enter it, and keeping them out would narrow what E10 reads. A D-n decision changes via config where §2.1 defines a field for it, otherwise by a logged change (D-72). §2.1 has no field for either today, so the revision would add the fields below, and each default keeps the decision as it stands. §3.3's adapter contract is untouched by all four: a new field is a `TrainConfig` field name like the others, and an adapter that cannot honour one reports it as not comparable.

### 5.1 First, the work that trains nothing

Eight rows of work, in the order of section 4.6: the six checks of item 15, then the three reads of item 10 in one row and the fourth nine-camera model of item 14. None waits on another, except that finalist 1's runs wait on check (c). Checks (a) and (b) come first because their results could take away the reason for finalists 1 and 2.

| | What it is | What it needs | What its result would change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| (a) | The notes' measures on openftgs-synth's checkpoints, where the cameras agree: the under-two-frames share, the far share of opacity and the base opacity's distribution (section 2.6), on M17's `default-395` runs of `kitchen`, `courtyard` and `studio`. The 5 cm is restated as 0.12·S and two frames at each scene's frame rate; the ground truth's dynamic masks stand in for the near side | The three checkpoints and their init points files under `<m17-runs>`; section 2.6's script; CPU minutes; no new source | It is the control section 4.2 lacks. If the shares there are like private scene A's (0.45 to 0.52 under two frames over the whole model; a far-5 cm share of 0.12 to 0.13 within the near side), the measures are the method's habit, a fall in them would not show that a disagreement was removed, and finalists 1 and 2 lose their reason as tests of H2 by removal. If they are clearly lower, the measures stand as criteria, and finalist 1's control on `kitchen` has its baseline |
| (b) | The same measures on private scene A's kept checkpoints, split by time centre at frame 80, after which five of the seven camera pairs disagree 2.2 to 4 times more (section 2.5) | The 45 checkpoints and their init points files; CPU minutes; no new source | If the shares are no higher after frame 80, the shell does not follow the disagreement in time, which weakens finalist 2 most, since it acts on the part that changes with the frame. If they are higher, the difference gives the size of effect to expect. The person is also closest in those frames, so a higher share alone does not separate the two causes |
| (c) | One correction per camera, nine in all, fitted on the background: to the epipolar residuals of all seven pairs, and to the agreement in depth of one camera's two partners (section 2.7). Then sections 2.5's and 2.7's measures again, on held-out frames | The matches section 2.5 saved, or the dense matcher again; up to one GPU hour; no new source | It is finalist 1's first step and decides what finalist 1 tests (section 5.3). A correction that is kept is fitted again for each held-out copy without that camera's images (section 5.3, D-66) and becomes the scene manifest every later arm trains on |
| (d) | Whether two models that differ by seed alone disagree where the shell is: `A_default_s0` against `A_default_s1` (section 2.6), by the distance from each primitive to the other model's nearest, for the near side's two populations, and by the difference of their renders from the sheet's request | The two checkpoints; two renders, GPU minutes; no new source | If the two seeds place the shell's primitives differently and the rest alike, two models' disagreement is a signal for the person's part, and rows E11 and E22 come back beside finalist 2. If they place it alike, that signal cannot see the shell |
| (e) | The colour differences between cameras, which nobody measured: the colours at the two ends of the matches section 2.5 admitted, per camera pair, with `kitchen` for the floor | The saved matches and the images of both scenes, read by scripts only; CPU minutes; no new source | A difference beyond `kitchen`'s brings back row E27, an affine colour correction in the training losses. None closes the part of section 3.6's setting that was left open. No finalist's place changes |
| (f) | The gradient of a depth channel in the colour pass against the same loss through Q-226's device, in U-12's configuration: the one path section 2.4 timed and did not run against anything | Seconds of GPU; no new source | It is what the adopted form of finalists 3 and 4 rests on, a depth output of `render` (sections 5.5 and 5.6). If it fails U-12's bounds, that form keeps a second pass, or the way z reaches `means` is looked at first. The tests go through the device and do not wait on it |
| Reads | FreeTimeGS++ in full (arXiv 2605.03337), which the owner allowed on 2026-11-16; SparseCam4D (arXiv 2603.26481); SSA-3DGS (arXiv 2607.05598). Each ends in a screening row of its family | A source-log row each, and a license row before any code is read; code that implements or extends FreeTimeGS stays unopened. Family E's cap goes from 24 to 26 full reads | FreeTimeGS++'s account of short-lived primitives bears on check (a)'s reading, and its initial velocity from flow gets the row no family gave it. SparseCam4D could add an alternative or a companion to finalist 2. SSA-3DGS bears on the placeholder pixels, beside row E13's known masks. No read removes a finalist (section 4.6, item 10) |
| Model | The fourth nine-camera model, the default configuration from a stride-1 init, behind a link the owner has allowed to be followed: its measures as section 2.6 took the others', and its sheet | The checkpoint and its init points file; one `ftgs render`, a GPU minute | It gives the nine-camera default its row. Beside `A_default_s0` it repeats, at stride 1, section 4.2's reading that the shell is the same with `cam0` in training or out of it, which rests on one pair of runs at stride 4. No finalist's place changes |

Together they need under two GPU hours and no training. They are not counted in section 4.5's hours, except check (c), which is finalist 1's first step there.

One more item of section 4.6's list, the owner's check of the placeholder-pixel sheets (item 12), was made on 2026-11-16, after review 1, so nothing here waits on it (sections 2.5 and 4.7). Section 5.2 says what it changes for the runs.

### 5.2 What every finalist's test shares

Sections 4.4 and 4.5 hold the criteria and the routes as review 1 left them: the criteria accepted, the routes and hours standing as this plan's proposal. Four things from them apply to every table below.

- **The route.** Finalist 1 needs none: a corrected scene manifest, then `ftgs init roma` and `ftgs train` unchanged. Finalists 2 to 4 are tried by a scratch script outside the package. `Trainer.step` calls `render` and `total_loss` by their names in `openftgs.train`, and a `Callback` runs only after a step, so the script puts wrappers in place of those two names at run time: one adds the terms, the other hands it the camera that Q-226's device needs. Before any arm counts, a run with every added weight at 0 must reproduce a plain `ftgs train` run within section 4.5's tolerance, since gsplat's backward is not bit-repeatable (Q-220).
- **The runs of an arm.** On private scene A, three: the copy that holds the centre camera out at seeds 0 and 1, and the copy that holds out the camera left of it at seed 0. On openftgs-synth, four: `kitchen` at seeds 0, 1 and 2 and `studio` at seed 0. All are 30,000 iterations of the default training configuration, from a stride-4 init on private scene A (D-21's stride, a setting of `ftgs init roma` and not of the training configuration) and from M17's init files on openftgs-synth (`docs/prd/validation-runs.md`, steps 1 and 16). The baseline is the same seven runs by plain `ftgs train`.
- **The measures and the criteria** are section 4.4's. Four points for whoever writes the scripts. The near-side far-5 cm share is taken against one fixed reference init points file for every arm of a comparison, where section 2.6's column is against each run's own file. And "run-to-run spread" on `kitchen` has two records an order of magnitude apart, 0.05 dB of PSNR from one repeat and a standard deviation of 0.55 to 1.54 dB over three seeds, which is why the guard there is SSIM₁ over three seeds and the geometry residual's shares. A mask that leaves placeholder pixels out of a score, or of a loss, is built with section 2.5's loose rule, the one the owner's check of the sheets confirmed, and a stored mask is checked against it first: they are then 0.300 % of `cam0`'s pixels, and the kept-pixel PSNR of a run that holds `cam0` out (Q-233) no longer waits for that check. And the tools' default rectangle, which item 13 keeps for the scores, crops the person a little on some images, part of a head or of an arm: a score on the crop misses that part, and the shell's measures, which are split by depth, do not depend on the rectangle.
- **What a prototype cannot show.** Through the device a depth or normal output costs half an iteration to one iteration more, where a channel of the colour pass would cost 2 % to 13 % (section 2.4), so no run here measures an adopted technique's cost: B-1 would, on the spec revision's implementation. And a loss on the device's render does not enter E10's g unless the script adds it; the plan leaves it out, so in these tests growth and relocation read the rendering loss alone.

### 5.3 Finalist 1: the cameras corrected once, before training

It is a preprocessing, not a field. Nothing in the training configuration turns it on, and a trained model knows of it only through the scene manifest it was trained from (section 4.3; rows E6 and E15).

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **What it would be** | A tool that reads a scene manifest and writes another, with each camera's `world_to_camera` corrected, and its `K` if the fit reaches a focal length, and with a record of what it changed. Two forms. A script under `scripts/`, beside `scripts/gt_depth.py`, which is outside §2.1's public API and comes with a documented step. Or a command of `ftgs`, which adds a line to §2.1's CLI block and a word to its `openftgs.cli` row: a change to a §2.1 signature, the owner's. Proposed: the script first, since the fit's form is not settled before check (c) |
| **Spec items it would touch, and how** | None of E1 to E17. `ftgs-scene` v1 already holds a `K` and a `world_to_camera` per camera (§2.2), so a corrected manifest is a valid v1 file. What the spec could add: a paragraph of §1.4 saying that training takes the manifest's cameras as exact and that a manifest may be corrected beforehand; and a `metadata` key that records the correction, a §2.2 addition. It meets these without changing them. §1.3 Initialization and E15's velocities run as they are, on the corrected cameras, and D-22's 2 px filter then keeps other matches: with section 2.5's turns two pairs kept more (67 % from 47 %, 73 % from 58 %) and two fewer (47 % from 58 %, 54 % from 68 %; section 2.7). D-27: S comes from the training cameras' centres, so it stays while each camera only turns about its own centre; a fit that moves a centre moves S, and with it E13's position rates and growth's split threshold of 0.01·S. D-66, whose reason is that no test image influences training: for a held-out copy, the training cameras' corrections are fitted without the held-out camera's images, and that camera is then corrected against them from its own images' background matches. That is one fit per held-out copy, two in all, where section 4.5's first step fits the nine cameras as one set; check (c) can still do so, to see whether one set reconciles the pairs, but a manifest that trains is fitted per copy. E16 and D-67: the scores at both poses of the held-out camera (the measures row) need no change, since `ftgs eval` reads a scene manifest without comparing its hash with the model's |
| **Fields it would add** | None in the training configuration. The tool's own settings are its arguments: the camera pairs, the certainty threshold (D-22's 0.5), which matches count as background, the robust loss. Check (c) fixes them |
| **Does the default leave E1 to E17 and the §3 tests untouched?** | Yes: no training code changes, and a manifest that is not corrected trains as today |
| **Prior maps, and the estimator** | None. The fit reads the dense matcher's matches, and the package already loads that matcher for `roma_init` (D-58), so §2.3 gains nothing |
| **The runs** | On private scene A, the three runs of section 5.2, each on its copy's corrected manifest with an init made on it, against the baseline's three. No arm on openftgs-synth, whose cameras are exact: there the tool's corrections have to stay at the fit's floor, which is a check. Optional control, on `kitchen`: each camera turned by a seeded 0.1° to 0.4°, then corrected by the tool; an init on each of the two manifests, and the default configuration at two seeds on each |
| **The measures and the criteria** | Section 4.4's. The measure it names is the near-side far-5 cm share (section 4.3), which should move only if the fit reaches the offset between pairs (the next row). It is taken against two reference files, triangulated from the same matches with the manifest's cameras and with the corrected ones, and counts as lower only against both. Each model is scored at both poses of the held-out camera, the manifest's and the corrected one (item 11b). On the control: the notes' measures, once check (a) has given them a baseline there, with the geometry residual's shares |
| **The route, and the first step** | No route of section 4.5's three: the package runs unchanged. The first step is check (c), with two outcomes. If the background's residual falls for every pair and the person's 4.0 % between pairs falls towards the 1.0 % to 1.6 % that section 2.7's fitted relation leaves, the finalist tests the cameras' whole part. If only the background's residual falls, it tests the background's part alone, and little change is expected on the person |
| **GPU hours** | 2; 2 more with the control |

### 5.4 Finalist 2: an alignment-tolerant pixel loss

The one finalist that is only a loss on today's primitives (section 4.3; row E16).

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Spec items it would touch, and how** | E7: with the field on, L_img is taken between each patch of Î and the best-matching patch of I within a window of shifts, with a penalty per pixel of shift. E7's composition, λ_img and λ_ssim are [P] and stay (λ_perc is D-9's). L_img's L1 is D-7's choice, which the field changes, keeping L1 as its default; D-8's SSIM and D-9's network change too if they read the shifted target (the last row but one). E9's composition stays. §2.1: `LossConfig` gains fields, a change to a listed signature, which is the owner's; `total_loss`'s signature stays, since the term reads Î and I alone. E10: g follows the shifted target with no further step, the loss being on the colour pass's render, so growth's test against τ_pos and relocation's score read the new loss; E11 and E17 are not touched. §2.4: the search over shifts is a new hot path, with a stage of its own for B-1. §3.1: a unit test of its own, such as a render that is the image shifted by k pixels costing k times the penalty, and a window of 0 giving E7's L1; U-7, U-8 and U-12 are not touched, since no render changes. §2.2 and §2.3: nothing |
| **Fields it would add** | `loss.align_window: int = 0`, the largest shift in pixels; `loss.align_patch: int`, the patch's side, whose default the test sets, since row E16 does not record the paper's; `loss.align_penalty: float = 0.01`, the cost per pixel of shift, the paper's (row E16). The last two are read only when `align_window` is above 0 |
| **Does the default leave E1 to E17 and the §3 tests untouched?** | Yes: at `align_window = 0` the term is E7's L1 and nothing else is computed |
| **Prior maps, and the estimator** | None |
| **The runs** | One arm on both sets of section 5.2, at the paper's window of 8 px and penalty of 0.01 (row E16), and a patch side that row E16 does not record, taken from the paper re-read under the later PRD, or else set in the first step. Nothing else differs from the default training configuration |
| **The measures and the criteria** | Section 4.4's. The measure it names is the under-two-frames share on the near side. Softer renders are its expected price, so the sharpness ratio and the sheets decide with it. On openftgs-synth it should do no harm: no residual share worse, SSIM₁ within 0.002 |
| **The route, and the first step** | A scratch script, with the wrapper of `total_loss` alone. First, time the search on one training image: no section measured it, and the hours below assume 1 to 2 times a default run |
| **What the test has to settle before a revision can be worded** | Whether SSIM and ꟻLIP read the shifted target too: D-8's window and D-89's network read whole images. The patch's side, which section 3.6's row does not record. Whether a shift is chosen per patch at every iteration, as the paper does, or held for some iterations |
| **GPU hours** | 2 to 4 |

### 5.5 Finalist 3: surface terms without a prior

Flattening, then depth–normal consistency in the form section 2.4 can supply (section 4.3; rows A1, A3 and A5).

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Spec items it would touch, and how** | E9: two terms added to L. L_flat, the mean over the primitives of the smallest of the three scales, reads `log_scales` and needs no render, as E8 needs none. L_nc compares, per pixel, the composited normal of the primitives with the normal of the rendered depth map. E7 stays. §2.1, four changes to listed signatures, all the owner's: `LossConfig` gains the weights and a start iteration; `RenderOutput` gains a depth and a normal field, and a plane distance if row A5's depth is used; `render` gains an argument that asks for them, beside `return_aux`, which asks for the gradient statistics only; `total_loss` gains them as an input, with the camera, which turns a depth map into normals. D-75 and D-76: the gsplat backend composites more channels through the stage functions it already calls (section 2.4, cells 1a, 4a and 8a), and D-76's background, composited outside the kernel, goes on the colour channels alone. The reference backend keeps z on the graph and composites the same vectors (column e). U-7 gains a line per new output, with a tolerance relative to the depth and a stated alpha cut; U-12 a case that weights accumulated depth and composited normals (section 2.4, part 3); U-8, on this section's reading, the new outputs with and without pre-culling. E10: as channels of the colour pass the new loss enters g with no further step, so the revision has to say whether it should. §1.3 Growth, E11 and E17 stay: E11 and E17 scale all three scales alike and so keep a primitive flat, and the clone-or-split test reads the largest scale, which flattening leaves alone (row A1). §2.4: 10 % to 13 % of an iteration as channels, for B-1 to measure. Untouched: §1.1, E5, E6 and `ftgs-if`, since the normal is derived from `quats` and `log_scales` and no quantity is added; §2.2 and §2.3; §3.3's adapter contract, while the new outputs stay inside training |
| **Fields it would add** | `loss.lambda_flat: float = 0.0`; `loss.lambda_normal_consistency: float = 0.0`; `loss.geometry_from: int = 0`, the first iteration at which the consistency term acts; `loss.geometry_depth`, "expected", the default and the depth section 2.4 supplies today, or "plane" |
| **Does the default leave E1 to E17 and the §3 tests untouched?** | Yes: a term whose weight is 0 is neither computed nor reported, as E9's terms are today, and `render` is asked for no new output |
| **Prior maps, and the estimator** | None |
| **The runs** | Two arms on both sets of section 5.2. The first: flattening alone. The second: flattening with the consistency term, starting at an iteration in the papers' range, 7,000 to 15,000 of 30,000 (row A3). The weights are set in a short calibration on one scene before the arms, whose form and hours are not settled and are not in section 4.5's count: PGSR's 100 for flattening stands beside another rendering loss than E7 |
| **The measures and the criteria** | Section 4.4's. The measure it names is the near-side far-5 cm share. H2 predicts that it falls with softer renders, or that the shell stays and turns flat; the sharpness ratio and the sheets tell the two from H1's outcome. On openftgs-synth a surface term is expected to do better: a lower share behind the surface, by more than 0.005 |
| **The route, and the first step** | A scratch script on Q-226's device: one pass for the normals, one for the plane distance or the depth. First, the numeric check of row A5's depth on one flat, slanted primitive, with its signed distance coded as the normal is. Check (f) stands behind the adopted form |
| **What the test has to settle before a revision can be worded** | Whether L_flat runs over all N or is weighted by σt(t) as E8 is (row A1). Which depth the consistency term reads. Whether the smallest-scale direction settles from today's spheres, or an oriented start is needed (row A10), which would add a key to `ftgs-init` and a field to `InitPoints`, a §2.1 signature |
| **GPU hours** | 6 |

### 5.6 Finalist 4: a normal prior, on top of finalist 3's flattening

H1 as the owner worded it, with a prior that fits no scale, shift or anchor (section 4.3; rows C7 and C8).

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Spec items it would touch, and how** | E9: two more terms. An L1 between the composited normals and the prior map (row C7). An L1 and a cosine between the rendered depth map's normals and the prior map, which may be weighted per pixel by how far the composited normal agrees with the prior (row C8). §2.1: `LossConfig` gains the weights; `RenderOutput` and `render` change as for finalist 3; `total_loss` gains the training image's prior map as an input. All are changes to listed signatures, the owner's. §2.2 `ftgs-scene`: a training image may carry `normal`, the path of a normal map of the image's size, in camera space and §1.4's axes, with `metadata.normal_source` saying what made the maps, as `mask_source` does for masks. That is a change of format, as a new optional key of v1 or as a v2, which the owner chooses; §2.2's readers, `read_scene_manifest` among them, ignore unknown optional keys, so code that predates a v1 key reads it as absent. The package would read the paths as it reads test images' masks today: `SceneManifest.masks` holds a path or None per record and `MultiViewVideo.load_mask` reads it, so a twin field and loader follow, a change to public API that Q-n rows record (Q-64 for `SceneManifest`, Q-65 for `load_mask`). The maps reach the GPU beside the frame cache (D-51), a cost B-6 would measure. §2.3: nothing while the maps are files. Untouched: E7, D-66, §1.1 and `ftgs-if`. U-7, U-8, U-12, E10, §1.3 Growth, E11 and E17 as for finalist 3 |
| **Fields it would add** | `loss.lambda_normal_prior: float = 0.0`; `loss.lambda_depth_normal_prior: float = 0.0`; `loss.normal_prior_confidence`, a float or None and None by default: row C8's γ, with None for no weight |
| **Does the default leave E1 to E17 and the §3 tests untouched?** | Yes, at weights of 0. A weight above 0 on a manifest whose training images carry no `normal` would be refused |
| **Prior maps, the estimator, and how the maps reach training** | One camera-space normal map per training image. Proposed: files that the scene manifest names, written by a tool outside the package, which is decision 3's supplied maps and puts no estimator in §2.3. The estimators, both passing: MoGe-2's `Ruicheng/moge-2-vitl-normal` on both scenes, and DAViD's `normal-model-vitl16_384.onnx` on private scene A's person (section 4.9; row 830 checked both again on 2026-11-16). DAViD's maps mean something on the person only, so the map's format needs a way to mark pixels that carry no prior. Not proposed: maps computed by the package, which would add an estimator, its weights and its runtime to §2.3, the owner's decision |
| **The runs** | One arm on both sets of section 5.2 with MoGe-2's maps, at finalist 3's flattening weight and the papers' own: 0.1 for row C7's L1, and 0.015 with γ = 0.005 for row C8's regularizer. Row C7's total-variation term, which section 4.3 does not mention, is left out of the arm by this section's choice, so that the arm adds the prior alone. It is read against finalist 3's two arms: what the prior adds to flattening, and the prior against consistency without one. Optional: one arm on private scene A with DAViD's maps |
| **The measures and the criteria** | As finalist 3. H2 predicts that each camera's normals describe that camera's version of the person, so that the loss settles between them and smooths |
| **The route, and the first step** | A scratch script on the device, with the maps written by a scratch environment, each package's and each weights file's license read and logged first (DAViD's files need an ONNX runtime). First, section 2.7's measurement repeated for normals: the estimator's normals turned into the world by the manifest's rotations and compared between the cameras of a pair on the person, with `kitchen` for the floor. On `kitchen` the same step checks the estimator's axes against §1.4's, with normals taken from the ground truth's depth |
| **GPU hours** | 4.5; 2 more with DAViD's arm |

### 5.7 The plan in order, and what it leaves open

| Step | What | Waits on | GPU hours |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The work that trains nothing (section 5.1) | The owner's go for a later PRD | Under 2 |
| 2 | The baseline, and the script's parity runs | Step 1's checks (a) and (b), which could change the measures | 2.5 |
| 3 | Finalist 1's runs, and its control on `kitchen` | Check (c), and the owner keeping finalist 1 after it | 1 to 3 |
| 4 | Finalist 2 | Step 2; on the corrected cameras if finalist 1's correction was kept | 2 to 4 |
| 5 | Finalist 3 | Step 2 | 6 |
| 6 | Finalist 4 | Finalist 3's flattening weight; the first step on normals | 4.5, or 6.5 |

Step 3's hours leave out check (c), which step 1 counts and section 4.5 counts in finalist 1's 2 hours, and include the control's optional 2. The four finalists with the baseline come to 17 to 19 GPU hours as section 4.5 counts them, and to 25 to 27 if a run on private scene A takes twice the notes' median. Each step's result goes to the owner before the next starts, and the owner can stop after any of them.

**What the plan leaves open.**

- **The weights.** Each arm runs at its paper's settings or after a short calibration; a sweep is one more arm per value at the same hours.
- **Combinations.** H2's own recommendation pairs a surface constraint with a way to absorb the disagreement. An arm that combines one finalist from each side follows the single results, and is not planned or counted.
- **The statistics.** Section 4.4's thresholds were accepted. If tests are wanted in their place, they are declared before any result, as #374's were.
- **The names and the wording.** Every field, key and argument above is a sketch. The spec revision that adopts a technique chooses them, and its implementation issue brings the tests of §3.1 that this section only names.
- **Where the results go.** Section 6 records that no finalist was cleared to train under this PRD; a later PRD that runs this plan reports there or in a document of its own.

## 6. The results

*Ticket #15's section.* For every finalist review 1 cleared to train, its results against the agreed criteria; or the record that none was cleared, or that a finalist waits for its spec revision and is tested later behind its field.

**No finalist was cleared to train under this PRD, so this section holds no result.** At review 1, on 2026-11-16, the owner answered that there are no tests in this PRD: nothing trains under PRD #1, the document stops at the test plan, and the recommendation rests on the evidence from papers and from the probes (section 4.7, lines R, H and K). Asked the same day which of the work that trains nothing should run before the test plan was written, the owner answered: none, straight to the test plan (section 4.7, items 10, 14 and 15). So no check, read or measurement was made after review 1. The six checks of section 4.6's item 15, the three reads of its item 10 and the fourth nine-camera model of its item 14 are the first rows of section 5.1, for a later PRD. The one thing settled after review 1 is the owner's check, by eye, of section 2.5's placeholder-pixel sheets, which fixed the share of `cam0`'s placeholder pixels at the loose rule's 0.300 % (sections 2.5 and 4.7).

**What stands as the evidence.**

- **The probes,** all run before review 1. Section 2.4: what gsplat 1.5.3's stage functions and the reference backend can composite, and Q-226's device, whose gradients agree between the backends within U-12's bounds. Section 2.5: the cameras' disagreement about the person against `kitchen`'s floor, the background's part of it, and the placeholder pixels. Section 2.6: the notes' shell measures on private scene A's 45 kept checkpoints, and the geometry residual on M17's checkpoints of openftgs-synth. Section 2.7: one depth estimator's maps lifted between cameras on both scenes. Section 4.2 reads the four against H2's premise.
- **The papers,** screened in sections 3.1 to 3.6: 85 techniques, consolidated in section 4.8, and the estimators against the bar, consolidated in section 4.9. The finalists' evidence is weighed in section 4.3.

**What does not exist.** Any test of H1 or of H2. Any result of a finalist, on private scene A or on openftgs-synth. The control section 4.2 lacks, the notes' measures on cameras that agree. A fit of all nine cameras as one set, and whether any fit reaches the 4 % by which two pairs place the person apart. Any normal estimator's output on either scene. The cost of finalist 2's patch search, and the cost of any technique as it would be adopted (section 4.5). A later PRD that runs section 5's plan reports its results here or in a document of its own (section 5.7).

## 7. The recommendation

*Ticket #15's section.* Which techniques to adopt, each as a field of the training configuration that defaults to off, or that none should be adopted.

**Adopt no technique against the shell now.** No spec revision should add a field for any of the four finalists before section 5's plan has run. None of them has been tried on cameras whose images disagree; the papers' evidence for each is thin, and comes from cameras that agree (section 4.3); and the probes show that private scene A's cameras disagree without showing that the shell is how training answers it (section 4.2). The next step is section 5's plan, in its order, as a later PRD (section 7.2). One technique can be adopted without that plan, because its case does not rest on the shell: known masks that leave the placeholder pixels out of the loss (row E13), behind a field that defaults to off, carried by the first spec revision that changes what a training image of `ftgs-scene` may carry or what `total_loss` reads (section 7.1).

Written on 2026-11-16, from sections 2.4 to 5 as merged. Nothing was run and no source was opened for it.

### 7.1 What is recommended now

- **No finalist is adopted.** A field that defaults to off is cheap to carry, but adopting one now would put in the spec a technique whose effect on the shell nobody has measured, on a premise the probes leave open. The result that would turn each finalist into a recommendation to adopt or to drop is in section 7.2.
- **Known masks left out of the loss (row E13): adopt, behind a field that defaults to off, in the first revision that touches the same places.** The argument:
  - The pixels are wrong. Magenta is the colour the generator was meant to replace (decision 4). By the loose rule, the one the owner's check of the sheets confirmed, they are 0.300 % of `cam0`'s pixels, all within the person's crop, against 20 to 52 per million on each of the other cameras (section 2.5).
  - E7 today asks the model to reproduce them. A mask removes a target known to be wrong while the surface under it stays supervised: the eight other cameras of the rig surround the same person (row 610), and the matcher admits next to nothing on those pixels (19 of 233,628 grid points, by the rule at m ≥ 64), so the init points do not come from them (section 2.5).
  - It needs no estimator, no render output and no camera trusted above another, and with its field off nothing changes. The score side exists already: section 5.2 leaves the same pixels out of a test view's kept-pixel PSNR (Q-233) where `cam0` is held out.
  - It will not touch the shell: the shell's measures are the same with `cam0` in training and out of it (section 4.2, one run each). Its gain is a fit of one camera without a known defect, not a step against the failure this PRD studies, so it does not justify a spec revision of its own. The places it changes, a key on training images in `ftgs-scene` and an input of `total_loss` (section 8), are the kind finalist 4 would change for its normal maps; whichever revision first changes them can carry it.

### 7.2 What is recommended to test, in order

Section 5's plan, as a later PRD, in section 5.7's order. Each result goes to the owner before the next step starts, so the owner can stop after any of them.

1. **The work that trains nothing** (section 5.1; under two GPU hours). Checks (a) and (b) come first: the notes' measures on openftgs-synth's checkpoints, where the cameras agree, and on private scene A split at frame 80. They are the cheapest way to lose finalists 1 and 2: if the measures are as high on cameras that agree, or do not rise where the disagreement does, the two lose their reason as tests of H2 by removal. Check (c) is finalist 1's first step. Then checks (d) to (f), the three reads and the fourth nine-camera model.
2. **The baseline and the script's parity runs** (section 4.5).
3. **Finalist 1,** the cameras corrected once before training, if check (c) gives the owner a reason to keep it. A correction that is kept becomes the manifest every later arm trains on.
4. **Finalist 2,** the alignment-tolerant pixel loss, on the corrected cameras if the correction was kept.
5. **Finalist 3,** flattening, then depth–normal consistency.
6. **Finalist 4,** a normal prior on top of finalist 3's flattening, after its first step on normals.

The disagreement side comes before the surface side because H2 predicts that a surface constraint alone trades the shell for blur, so a surface finalist's result cannot be read until it is known whether the cameras are corrected (section 4.6, line H); and each finalist comes after the one it builds on. The combination H2 calls for, one finalist from each side, follows the single results and is neither planned nor counted (section 5.7).

**What would turn each finalist into a recommendation to adopt or to drop.** The criteria are section 4.4's, which review 1 accepted, read as its paragraph "How a result would be read" says. On private scene A, the measure the finalist names has moved (both of its seeds beyond both of the baseline's, and the means more than 0.01 apart), the owner judges its sheets no worse with their labels hidden, the sharpness ratio has not fallen beyond what two seeds give, and no test-view score has fallen by more than 0.3 dB. On openftgs-synth, no residual share is worse by more than 0.005, and SSIM₁ holds within 0.002. A measure that moves while the renders turn soft is the trade H2 predicts, not a pass.

| Finalist | Adopt when | Drop when | If its measure moves and the renders turn soft |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. The cameras corrected once (a tool and a corrected manifest, not a field) | Check (c) lowers the background's residual for every pair on held-out frames, and on the corrected manifests the near-side far-5 cm share is lower against both reference files, with no harm by the other criteria at either pose of the held-out camera. It is then adopted as section 5.3's script and documented step | Check (c) does not lower the background's residual on held-out frames, or the share does not move. A correction that holds on held-out frames is still a better manifest, and keeping it for that is the owner's call | Not expected: it constrains no surface |
| 2. The alignment-tolerant pixel loss | The near-side under-two-frames share moves, with no harm by the other criteria. Adopted behind `loss.align_window`, 0 by default (section 5.4) | The share does not move: the shell is not a reconciliation, or the disagreement is not a shift of a few pixels rigid over a patch (section 4.3) | The disagreement side of the combined arm, not an adoption by itself |
| 3. Surface terms without a prior | The near-side far-5 cm share moves with the renders sharp, with no harm on openftgs-synth, where a lower share behind the surface is expected (section 4.4). Adopted behind its weights, 0 by default (section 5.5). The first arm, flattening alone, is a step and predicts nothing about the shell | The shell stays and turns flat, thin and aligned with its own depth, H2's other outcome (section 4.3) | The surface side of the combined arm |
| 4. A normal prior | It moves the named measure beyond finalist 3's two arms, with the renders sharp and no harm on openftgs-synth. Adopted behind its weights, 0 by default, with the maps as files (section 5.6), once its estimator's verdict has been checked again at the publisher (section 3.3) | It adds nothing to finalist 3's flattening | H2's prediction for it, the loss settling between the cameras' versions of the person (section 4.3): the combined arm |

**Open with the owner, and not settled here.** Whether finalist 1's fit is made once for the nine cameras (section 4.5) or once per held-out copy without the held-out camera's images, for D-66's reason (section 5.3). And whether finalist 4's arm takes the total-variation term of row C7 (section 5.6).

### 7.3 What needs no spec change

Each of these runs on today's spec and package. None is recommended for use as it stands, and each says why.

- **A corrected scene manifest** (finalist 1). `ftgs-scene` v1 holds a `K` and a `world_to_camera` per camera, so a corrected manifest is a valid v1 file, and `ftgs init roma` and `ftgs train` run on it unchanged (section 5.3). No such manifest exists: the fit is check (c), and how it is made is open (section 7.2).
- **Init points supplied as an `ftgs-init` file.** Positions, velocities and durations from any producer, prior maps included, skip initialization's steps 1 to 6 (§1.3; section 3.4). A start lifted from per-camera depth would cover the 73 % of the person's kept pixels that have no admitted match, and it inherits the 4 % between pairs through its alignment until the cameras agree (section 2.7), so it comes after finalist 1 (section 4.3, last table). MotionCrafter, the owner's search seed, fails the bar and no estimator of scene flow passes, so its outputs could come only as files from a user with the right to run it (section 3.3). An oriented or anisotropic start needs a key `ftgs-init` v1 lacks.
- **Other values of `loss.lambda_perc` and `loss.perc_net`,** among D-89's networks. The notes tried one, `vgg16_oxford`, with no gain (section 2.1; rows E1 and E2).

### 7.4 What is recommended against

| What | Rows | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Weights or masks taken from the residual or from pretrained features | E3, E4, E12, E19 | They would mark the person, in every camera and frame, as the content that disagrees, and remove it (section 3.6, reading 2) |
| Depth priors that pass through an alignment to triangulated or rendered depth | C1, C12, the absolute term of C5 | They inherit the 4 % by which two pairs place the person; one scale and shift per image leave the person 4.8 % off and the background 13.5 % off, with opposite signs (section 2.7) |
| Depth priors taken within one image | C2 to C4 | Their measured gains vanish as views are added: DNGaussian gains 2.7 dB with 3 views and none with 9, and private scene A trains on eight or nine (row C2) |
| Multi-view geometric consistency terms | A6 | They assume cameras that agree within about a pixel, and PGSR's own ablation shows its term doing harm where views do not match; the person's matches are 1.84 px apart at the median (sections 2.5 and 3.1) |
| More pressure on the base opacity | B1, B2, B5, B6 | They are neighbours of `loss.lambda_reg` and `reloc.dead_opacity`, which the notes took from no gain to collapse, and the base opacity does not tell the shell from the surface: medians of 0.12 and 0.18 (sections 2.1 and 2.6) |
| Agreement of velocity or rigidity between nearest neighbours | D8, with row D7's comparison | Nothing measured implicates the velocities: starting them at zero left the notes' measures where they were (section 2.6). Rigidity over nearest neighbours lowered PSNR by 0.6 to 2 dB in the one controlled comparison read (row D7) |
| MotionCrafter as an estimator in the package | C15 | Its weights are licensed for academic use and are not intended for use in the European Union (section 3.3) |

The other techniques that a survey flagged and section 4.3 did not propose are not recommended now; section 4.3's last table gives, for each, the result that would bring it back.

### 7.5 Where H1 and H2 stand

- **H1 is untested.** Nothing was trained with a surface constraint. The papers' evidence for the forms that can be supplied today is thin: three figures for 3D Gaussians with the smallest-scale normal, all on static scenes whose cameras agree, and for normal priors only figures with estimators that fail the bar (section 4.3, finalists 3 and 4). No paper read tests a surface term on several cameras whose images disagree at one time. Half of H1 as the owner worded it meets a measured obstacle on this scene: a depth prior made per camera lands where its alignment puts it, and inherits the 4 % between pairs (section 2.7). The other half, normal priors, meets no measured obstacle, because no normal estimator was run.
- **H2's premise holds; H2 is untested.** The cameras disagree about the person, 21 to 99 times the measure's floor per pair, and part of that is the cameras' own error (section 2.5). A depth prior made per camera inherits the disagreement in position whole; in shape, the estimator's own error hides it (section 2.7). That the shell is how training reconciles the disagreement has no evidence for or against: no probe located the shell's primitives against the disagreement, and the measures were never taken on cameras that agree (section 4.2). One figure cuts against its simplest reading: what was measured is 2 to 10 mm across the epipolar line and about 4 cm in depth between pairs, on the part of the person that can be matched, where the primitives far from every init point on the person's side lie 7.4, 17.0 and 37.6 cm from the nearest one at their quartiles (section 2.6, the default checkpoint; this section uses those measured figures rather than the notes' 5 to 25 cm). Family E's papers report H2's diagnosis in other settings: blur, redundant points, per-frame overfitting and conflicting gradients from generated views that disagree (section 3.6, finding 2).

### 7.6 The research questions, answered

1. **Which techniques make primitives follow a surface, and what does each need?** (Sections 2.4, 3.1, 3.2 and 4.8.) On today's primitives: the flattening loss, which reads the scales only, and depth–normal consistency, which needs a composited normal and the normal of the rendered depth map. Both can be supplied for 3D Gaussians as channels of the colour pass, or through Q-226's device without an API change. The forms on median depth or the pairwise distortion cannot, since gsplat 1.5.3 computes those only in its 2DGS kernel. Flat primitives are a change to a quantity, with a second E6. The multi-view terms assume agreement within a pixel. Family B's regularizers act on the base opacity, the duration or the scales, and the base opacity does not tell the shell from the surface.
2. **Which prior maps supervise or initialize them, with which losses, and which estimators clear the bar?** (Sections 2.7, 3.3, 3.4 and 4.9.) Of section 3.3's 68 weights rows, 18 pass. Every kind of map but scene flow has a passing estimator, among them Depth Anything V2 Small for depth, MoGe-2's normal files and Marigold V2's for normals, and DAViD's models for people. Losses taken within one image shape a surface and cannot place it; losses through an alignment place it at whatever they were aligned to, and on this scene inherit the 4 % between pairs; the measured gains of depth priors shrink as views are added. Every technique also works from maps supplied as files, which needs the path per training image that `ftgs-scene` v1 lacks.
3. **How do dynamic-scene methods keep a surface coherent over time, and what would that ask of today's primitives?** (Section 3.5.) By identity that lasts, a canonical state or the same primitives at every timestep, which today's primitives lack. Orientation that changes with time is added per primitive or from a field, and no paper read reports what turning buys in geometry; with durations of one to four frames a constant orientation is right for most of a primitive's life (section 3.1). Three asks leave the primitive as it is: agreement of velocity between neighbours, a loss that lengthens durations, and 4DSurf's agreement of normal motion with the change of depth, which needs new render outputs.
4. **How do methods train on cameras whose images disagree, none of them trusted, some of whose pixels are known to be wrong?** (Section 3.6.) The nearest published settings answer on the generator's side, or lean on a real view or a reference view, which private scene A lacks. By part of the disagreement: the background's part by a correction per camera (finalist 1); the person's part by a deformation per camera, which needs a reference, by a lower weight, where signals from residuals or features would remove the person and two other signals trust no camera, or by a moved target (finalist 2); the placeholder pixels by known masks (row E13); colour, which nobody measured, by a correction per camera.
5. **Can each shortlisted technique be a field of the training configuration that defaults to off and leaves E1 to E17 and the §3 tests untouched?** (Sections 5.3 to 5.6 and 8.) Finalists 2 to 4 and row E13: yes, each behind fields whose defaults keep today's behaviour and today's tests. Each changes signatures §2.1 lists, which is a spec change the owner makes: `LossConfig` for all four; `RenderOutput`, `render` and `total_loss` for finalists 3 and 4; `total_loss` for row E13. Finalist 4 and row E13 also change `ftgs-scene`. Finalist 1 is not a field: it is a preprocessing that changes no spec item.

### 7.7 The owner's answer at review 2

Review 2 took place on 2026-11-16, in session, between the owner and the orchestrating session, on sections 6 to 9 as drafted. The answers, as the owner gave them (written here by the orchestrating session):

- **The recommendation of section 7.1 is accepted:** no technique against the shell is adopted now, and section 5's test plan is to run as a later PRD, starting with the work that trains nothing. **Confirmed** (2026-11-16).
- **Row E13, known masks for the placeholder pixels,** is adopted with the next related spec revision: one that already touches what a training image in `ftgs-scene` may carry or what `total_loss` reads. No revision is made for it alone. **Confirmed** (2026-11-16).
- **Finalist 1's camera fit** is made once per held-out copy, without the held-out camera's images, as section 5.3 proposes for D-66's reason; a joint fit of all nine cameras, as section 4.5 first described it, is not the plan's. **Answered** (2026-11-16).
- **Finalist 4's arm** leaves out the total-variation term of its source row, as section 5.6 has it, so that its result reads as the prior's effect. **Confirmed** (2026-11-16).

What is not decided here: the wording of a spec revision, which the owner makes as the next lettered revision on an issue of its own ("Editing the spec" in `docs/agents/guidelines.md`), and the later PRD itself.

## 8. What adopting it would change

*Ticket #15's section.* For each recommended technique, over the PRD's list: §1.1 and E5 to E6 if a quantity or the primitive's shape changes, and E1 to E4 if orientation varies with time; E7 and E9 for new loss terms; §1.3 Growth, E10, E11 and E17 for constrained primitives; §1.3 Initialization and E15 for initialization from prior maps; §2.1's `RenderOutput`, `render` and `LossConfig`; §2.2's `ftgs-scene` and `ftgs-if`; §2.3 for any estimator, weights or dependency; U-7, U-8 and U-12; §3.3's adapter contract; §2.4 for cost.

**None of the five techniques section 7 recommends adopting or testing changes §1.1, E1 to E6, `ftgs-if` or §3.3's adapter contract, and none adds a package or weights to §2.3 while prior maps come as files.** What they change is losses, render outputs and, for two of them, what a training image of `ftgs-scene` may carry. The five are the four finalists, which section 7.2 recommends testing, and known masks (row E13), which section 7.1 recommends adopting. Sections 5.3 to 5.6 sketch the finalists against the spec in detail; the table cites them and does not repeat them. Every change to a signature §2.1 lists, or to public API a Q-n row records, is a spec change the owner makes, in the next lettered revision ("Editing the spec" in `docs/agents/guidelines.md`); nothing here is a Q-n candidate. Field and key names are placeholders the revision chooses (section 5).

| E1–E4, if orientation varies with time | Unchanged | Unchanged | Unchanged | Unchanged | Unchanged |
| E7 and E9, for new loss terms | Unchanged | E7's L_img is taken against the best-matching patch of I within a window of shifts, at a penalty per pixel of shift: D-7's choice of L1 changes, with L1 as the default. E7's composition, λ_img and λ_ssim stay; whether D-8's SSIM and D-89's network read the shifted target is for the test to settle. E9 unchanged (section 5.4) | E7 unchanged. E9 gains L_flat, the smallest scale per primitive, and L_nc, the composited normal against the normal of the rendered depth map. Whether L_flat is weighted by σt(t) as E8 is, and which depth L_nc reads, are for the test (section 5.5) | E7 unchanged. E9 gains an L1 of the composited normals against the prior map (row C7) and an L1 and a cosine of the depth map's normals against it, optionally weighted by their agreement (row C8) (section 5.6) | Each of E7's three terms leaves the masked pixels out: L1 per pixel; for SSIM, whose 11 × 11 valid window mixes kept and masked pixels (D-8), and for the perceptual network, which reads whole images (D-89), the revision chooses how (section 3.6, reading 2). E11 and E17 unchanged (section 5.4) | Growth, E11 and E17 unchanged: E11 and E17 scale the three scales alike and keep a primitive flat, and the clone-or-split test reads the largest scale, which flattening leaves alone. At a window of 0 the term is E7's L1 and nothing else is computed: untouched | `loss.lambda_flat: float = 0.0`, `loss.lambda_normal_consistency: float = 0.0`, `loss.geometry_from: int = 0`, `loss.geometry_depth`, "expected" by default. At weights of 0 nothing is computed and `render` is asked for no new output: untouched | `loss.lambda_normal_prior: float = 0.0`, `loss.lambda_depth_normal_prior: float = 0.0`, `loss.normal_prior_confidence`, None by default. Untouched at weights of 0; a weight above 0 on a manifest whose training images carry no `normal` is refused | One switch, such as `loss.train_masks: bool = False`. Off, no mask is read and E7 is as it is: untouched |

**What the revision would weigh across the columns.**

- **The tags** (§0, D-72). Finalist 2 changes D-7's choice of L1; finalists 3 and 4 add terms to E9, whose composition is D-37's; row E13 changes which pixels D-7's, D-8's and D-89's terms cover. Each is a D-n decision, changed through the new field. The one [P] item in question is E10's g (P Eq. 7), which reads E9's whole loss: with finalists 2 to 4 and row E13 the new or masked terms reach g through the colour pass, and the revision says whether they should, with its reason logged.
- **Shared changes.** Finalists 3 and 4 need the same render outputs, so one revision of `RenderOutput` and `render` serves both, and check (f) comes before it. Finalist 4 and row E13 change `ftgs-scene`'s training images in the same way, a path per training image read beside the frames and an input of `total_loss`, so one revision of the format can serve both.
- **A combination.** If the combined arm H2 calls for is what a later test supports, its changes are the union of its two columns.

## 9. The sources

*Ticket #15's section.* The clean-room record in full, completing the opening's: the source-log rows of every ticket of the PRD, what was read only as documentation, what was not opened, and any incident.

**Every source behind this document has a row in `docs/prd/source-log.md`: rows 491 to 830, 340 in all. No row is an Incident row.** Five rows record hits deliberately left unopened, because they looked like code that implements or extends FreeTimeGS. Third-party source code was read in two places, both under Apache-2.0. No technique's own repository was opened beyond its license file and README: the techniques of sections 3.1 to 3.6 are taken from their papers, with gsplat 1.5.3's code read beside them on three points (rows 653, 660, 804). The opening's "Clean-room record" is the short form of this section.

Written on 2026-11-16 from the source log, the integration branch's merge commits, the PRD's comments and the orchestrating session's record of the run, a working note outside the repository. A fact that only that record holds is marked "(the run's record)".

### 9.1 The rules

- **CLAUDE.md's guidelines rules,** which replace spec §0 on sources for this fork (owner rule, 2026-11-13), and the PRD's "Clean room for the search", which section 3 summarizes. Each project's license file is read before anything else of it; source code only under a permissive license that allows commercial use; a repository under a copyleft, non-commercial or missing license is documentation only; code that implements or extends FreeTimeGS, and EasyVolcap, are never opened; gsplat is read at 1.5.3 only; weights' terms come from their publisher; no repository-indexing tools; third-party text is paraphrased.
- **The owner's rulings during the run,** recorded in the opening: papers that cite FreeTimeGS and describe or use their authors' own reproduction of it may be read and cited, set-up included, and FreeTimeGS++ may be read whole (2026-11-16; section 9.6); a third-party paper's published figure on SelfCap is a citation, not material derived from SelfCap (2026-11-16); decision 5 covers image content given as numbers (2026-11-16).
- **Searches named FreeTimeGS,** as CLAUDE.md allows, to find the papers that cite it: the recency rounds of sections 3.1, 3.2, 3.4, 3.5 and 3.6 read their list. No result that looked like an implementation was opened (section 9.5).
- **Private scene A.** The rows call it by its alias, and the planning row on the generator's model card leaves out its name and address (row 496). That its name, paths, generator and images appear in no row, commit, search query or issue is the run's record.

A repository was opened for its license file, read first, to fill a code-license column, and at most for its README, which is documentation and allowed under every license class. Where a technique needed more than its paper, gsplat 1.5.3's code was read for it (rows 653, 660, 804, above).

### 9.6 Weights, and the reproduction question

**How weights' terms were read** (section 3.3, "How the bar is applied"). Per weights file, from its publisher only: the model card or license file of the weights' repository, or the publisher's README or release page, with its address, the commit it was read at and where the statement stands. Every request was anonymous; a gated repository was recorded as gated and its gate left alone. Where two statements of one publisher disagree, or a file is fine-tuned from another model, the stricter statement decides. A verdict holds for the commit and date of its row: the orchestrating session checked again the terms of the estimators the probes and finalist 4 rely on (rows 607 and 830). Review 1 screened out the "unclear" rows and kept the "fails as stated" rows failing (section 4.7, item 8). One weights file was downloaded, Depth Anything V2 Small's, without credentials into a scratch directory and loaded with `weights_only=True` (row 618); RoMa's and DINOv2's weights ran from the existing cache (rows 613, 622). No weights file was opened as text, committed or redistributed.

**Papers that reproduce FreeTimeGS.**

- **Two passages read outside the rule.** Ticket #10 read TRiGS's Appendix B, which describes its authors' reproduction of FreeTimeGS, by accident, through a line range wider than it meant; its row says so and that nothing of it is taken (row 771). Ticket #11 read GraphiXS's paragraph on an experiment that upgrades a FreeTimeGS implementation, and two rows of its table, before the interim rule was given, and recorded no figure from them (row 820).
- **The interim rule, 2026-11-15,** set by the orchestrating session until the owner answered: sections that describe a reproduction's set-up are left unread, no set-up is recorded, and a cell whose evidence rests on an experiment on a reproduction says "rests on the authors' reproduction of FreeTimeGS". Ticket #10 had left FreeTimeGS++'s "Reimplementation Details" and its appendices A to D unread on purpose (row 766), and ticket #11, under the rule, read only its section on colour correction (row 829).
- **The owner's ruling, 2026-11-16:** such papers may be read and cited freely, the reproduction's set-up included; FreeTimeGS++ may be read whole; code that implements or extends FreeTimeGS stays unopened. The marks stay where they are, as information. Neither row 771 nor row 820 is labelled Incident; the orchestrating session had proposed relabelling row 771 as one (the PRD's comment of 2026-11-16), and the ruling allows what it records. Nothing was re-read after the ruling, since the owner then answered that nothing further runs in this PRD: FreeTimeGS++ in full is one of section 5.1's reads.

### 9.7 What went wrong or differently

- **A forbidden read of Claude Code's own files.** Ticket #3's subagent opened one file under `~/.claude/`, a tool output the harness had saved there that held lines 1 to 190 of this document, which CLAUDE.md forbids. It was reported to the owner the same day, and a rule was added to the subagents' brief (the run's record).
- **A thread budget exceeded.** Ticket #3 ran one command at about five cores against a budget of four (the run's record).
- **Rows logged late,** after a usage cut or a run, each saying so in its own text: rows 575 to 594 (ticket #6), 640 to 642 (ticket #7), 721 (ticket #9) and 759 to 763 (ticket #10, covering 17 abstracts). The run's record adds ticket #3's later rows, written after its reading pass and its runs, and ticket #4's and ticket #12's rows for data that scripts read.
- **A branch rebuilt.** Ticket #5 rebuilt its branch once before its merge to take a few file names of the notes' folder out of a commands table; no scene name or absolute path was ever committed (the run's record).
- **Usage cuts and the subagents.** Account usage limits cut subagents off several times from 2026-11-14 to 16, and work resumed from commits and scratch notes. From 2026-11-15 the owner asked for one subagent at a time, and on 2026-11-16 moved the remaining subagents to another model (the run's record).
- **No `tdd`.** No §3 test belongs to this PRD and only the document and the source log changed, so the `tdd` skill was not used; each ticket's acceptance criteria stood in.
- **Private scene A's images** were never opened with an image reader by any agent, and nothing derived from them left the machine: scripts read them (rows 614, 620). The owner checked the placeholder-pixel sheets by eye on 2026-11-16 (section 2.5).
- **Requests to the web** carried no name, email, project name or anything about private scene A (the run's record); the rows say, per source, that model cards and license files were fetched without credentials.
